\Delta_{-}^{l^{\prime}}=\{\alpha\in\Delta_{-}:-\gamma_{n+1}\leq\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}\}
.

Combining (
3.4.19
) and (
3.4.20
), using the definition of the circular normal ordering of the root system
\Delta
associated to
s
, Lemmas
1.6.6
,
3.4.4
and commutation relations between one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots one can obtain

n_{-}^{-1}uy=n_{-}^{-1}ns^{1}n^{\prime}n^{\prime\prime}s^{2}n^{\prime\prime\prime}y=ks^{1}gs^{2}n^{\prime\prime\prime}y,g\in N_{\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}},k\in N_{-}.

(3.4.21)

By Proposition
1.6.1

s^{2}\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\subset\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus\left(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{s^{2}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0}\right)\subset\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{0},

and hence

s^{2}\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\subset\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\subset\left(\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus\left(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{s^{2}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0}\right)\right)\cap\left(\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{0}\right),

(3.4.22)

Now the minimal segment
\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}
can be represented as the following disjoint union

\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}=\left(\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0})\right)\cup\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}.

(3.4.23)

Note that since the decomposition
s=s^{1}s^{2}
is reduced we have

s^{1}(\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0}))\subset\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0})\subset\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{0}.

(3.4.24)

Observe that the segments
\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
,
\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0})
are minimal with respect to the circular normal ordering on
\Delta
associated to
s
. Thus from (
3.4.23
) and Lemma
3.4.4
we deduce that the element
g\in N_{\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}}
from formula (
3.4.21
) can be uniquely decomposed as the product

g=g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}
, where
g^{\prime}\in N_{-\left(\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus(\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0})\right)}
, and
g^{\prime\prime}\in N_{-\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}}
. By (
3.4.22
), (
3.4.24
) we have
s^{1}g^{\prime}(s^{1})^{-1}\in N_{-}
and
(s^{2})^{-1}g^{\prime\prime}s^{2}\in M_{-}
, and (
3.4.21
) takes the form

n_{-}^{-1}uy=ks^{1}g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}s^{2}n^{\prime\prime\prime}y=ks^{1}g^{\prime}(s^{1})^{-1}s(s^{2})^{-1}g^{\prime\prime}s^{2}n^{\prime\prime\prime}y=k^{\prime}s\widehat{n}y,\widehat{n}\in M_{-},k^{\prime}\in N_{-}.

(3.4.25)

The element
\widehat{n}y
belongs to the subgroup
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
,
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha\in\Delta:\gamma_{l^{\prime}}<\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}\}
. The images of all roots from the set
(\Delta^{\prime}\setminus\Delta_{0})\cap\Delta_{+}
under the action of
s
belong to
\Delta_{-}
. The set complimentary to
(\Delta^{\prime}\setminus\Delta_{0})\cap\Delta_{+}
in
\Delta^{\prime}
is
-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\cup(\Delta_{0}\cap\Delta_{+})
. Therefore using the decomposition of elements
N_{\Delta^{\prime}}
as products of elements from one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots and recalling Lemma
3.4.4
one can get
\widehat{n}y=y^{\prime}z_{+}m
, where
z_{+}\in Z_{+}=Z\cap N_{+}
,
sy^{\prime}s^{-1}\in N_{-}
,
m\in M_{-}
, and

n_{-}^{-1}uy=k^{\prime\prime}sz_{+}m=k^{\prime\prime}z_{+}^{\prime}sm,m\in M_{-},k^{\prime\prime}\in N_{-},z_{+},z_{+}^{\prime}=sz_{+}s^{-1}\in Z_{+}.

(3.4.26)

Hence
n_{-}^{-1}uy\in N_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}
.

Now we show that

N_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}=N_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}=(N_{-}\cap N)Z_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}=(N_{-}\cap N)Z_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}.

Note that the elements of the subgroup
N_{-}\cap\overline{N}
are transformed to
M_{-}
by the conjugation by
s^{-1}
. Therefore using a decomposition of elements
N_{-}
into products of elements from one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots and Lemma
3.4.4
one can obtain that any
k^{\prime\prime}\in N_{-}
can be represented in the form
k^{\prime\prime}=\overline{n}z_{-}k^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
\overline{n}\in N\cap N_{-}
,
z_{-}\in Z_{-}=Z\cap N_{-}
,
k^{\prime\prime\prime}\in N_{-}\cap\overline{N}
,
s^{-1}k^{\prime\prime\prime}s\in M_{-}
. Therefore for any
m\in M_{-},z_{+}\in Z_{+}
, we have

k^{\prime\prime}smz_{+}=\overline{n}z_{-}sm^{\prime}z_{+},\overline{n}\in N\cap N_{-},m^{\prime}=s^{-1}k^{\prime\prime\prime}sm\in M_{-},z_{-}\in Z_{-},

and hence
N_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}\subset(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}
. The opposite inclusion is obvious. Thus
N_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}
. Similarly one obtains
N_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}=N_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}
and obviously
(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}\subset(N_{-}\cap N)sZM_{-}\subset NsZN
.

Next we prove that for any
n_{-}\in N_{-},x\in\mathfrak{h}
and
y\in N_{\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}}
we have

h_{-}^{-1}n_{-}^{-1}uyh_{+}\in(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}\subset NsZN
, where
h_{\pm}=e^{r_{\pm}^{s}x}
, i.e.
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))\subset(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}\subset NsZN
.

Let
H^{\prime}\subset H
be the subgroup corresponding to the Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{h}
. Recall that we denote by
H_{0}\subset H
the subgroup corresponding to the orthogonal complement
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\perp}
of
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{h}
with respect to the symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}
. Note that
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\perp}
is the space of fixed points for the action of
s
on
\mathfrak{h}
. We obviously have
H=H^{\prime}H_{0}
. From the definition of
r_{\pm}^{s}
it follows that for any
h_{0}\in H_{0}
and
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
elements
h_{+}=h_{0}h^{\prime}
and
h_{-}=h_{0}^{-1}s(h^{\prime})
are of the form
h_{\pm}=e^{r_{\pm}^{s}x}
for some
x\in\mathfrak{h}
and all elements
h_{\pm}
are obtained in this way.

Next observe that the space
(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}
is invariant with respect to
the following action of the subgroup of
H\times H
formed by elements of the form
(h_{+},h_{-})=(h_{0}h^{\prime},h_{0}^{-1}s(h^{\prime}))
:

(h_{+},h_{-})\circ L=h_{-}^{-1}Lh_{+},h=h_{+}=h_{0}h^{\prime},h_{-}=h_{0}^{-1}s(h^{\prime}).

(3.4.27)

Indeed, let
L=vz_{-}skwz_{+},~v\in(N\cap N_{-}),w\in M_{-},z_{\pm}\in Z_{\pm},k\in H_{0}
be an element of
(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}
. Then

(h_{+},h_{-})\circ L=h_{-}^{-1}vz_{-}h_{-}h_{-}^{-1}skh_{+}h_{+}^{-1}wz_{+}h_{+}=h_{-}^{-1}vz_{-}h_{-}skh_{0}^{2}h_{+}^{-1}wz_{+}h_{+}

(3.4.28)

since
s^{-1}h_{-}^{-1}s=h_{0}h^{\prime-1}
. The r.h.s. of the last equality belongs to
(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}
because
H
normalizes
(N\cap N_{-})
,
M_{-}
and
Z_{\pm}
.

Comparing action (
3.4.27
) with (
3.4.13
) and recalling that for any
n_{-}\in N_{-}
and
y\in N_{\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}}
one has
n_{-}^{-1}uy\in(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}\subset(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}
we deduce

q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))\subset(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}
.

Similarly, using the fact that
H
normalizes the subgroups
N_{-},N\cap N_{-},M_{-}
and
Z_{\pm}
of
G
and recalling that for any
n_{-}\in N_{-}
and
y\in N_{\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}}
we also have
n_{-}^{-1}uy\in N_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}=N_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sM_{-}Z_{+}
, one can show that
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))\subset N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}=N_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}M_{-}Z_{+}=(N\cap N_{-})Z_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}=(N\cap N_{-})sZ_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}\subset NsZN
, where the last inclusion follows from the inclusions
Z_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}\subset Z
,
M_{-}\subset N
.

The set
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is not Zariski closed in
G
. But by Proposition
1.3.1

NsZN
is Zariski closed in
G
. Therefore the Zariski closure
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is contained in
NsZN
.

∎

Now we are in a position to describe the closure
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
, the quotient
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})
and the algebra
W^{s}(G)
.

Theorem 3.4.5
.

Suppose that the numbers
t_{i}
defined in (
3.4.5
) are not equal to zero for all
i
. Let
N_{s}^{\prime}\subset N_{-}\cap N_{s}
be the subgroup generated by one–parameter subgroups corresponding to the roots
\alpha\in\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}
satisfying the condition
-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}<\alpha
,
M_{-}^{s}=M_{-}\cap N_{s}
.
Then
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}=N_{-}{s}ZM_{-}=N_{-}{s}ZM_{-}^{s}
is invariant under conjugations by elements of
M_{-}
, the conjugation action of
M_{-}
on
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is free, the quotient
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})
is a smooth variety isomorphic to
N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}\simeq\Sigma_{s}=sZN_{s}
,
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})\simeq N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}
. Moreover, the conjugation action

M_{-}\times N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}\rightarrow N_{-}{s}ZM_{-}

(3.4.29)

is an isomorphism of varieties, and hence the algebra
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{C}}[M_{-}]\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}]
.

The Poisson algebra
W^{s}(G)
is isomorphic to the Poisson algebra of regular
functions on
N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}
,
W^{s}(G)=\mathbb{C}[\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})]=\mathbb{C}[N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}]\simeq\mathbb{C}[\Sigma_{s}]
. Thus the algebra
W_{\mathcal{B}}^{s}(G)
is a non–commutative deformation of the algebra of regular functions on the transversal slice
\Sigma_{s}\simeq N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}
.

Proof.

Firstly, as we observed in Lemma
3.4.2
the preimage
\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u)
is locally stable under the (locally defined) dressing action of
M_{-}
, and hence

q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))\subset N{s}ZN
is (locally) stable under the action of
M_{-}\subset N
on
N{s}ZN
by conjugations. Since the conjugation action of
N
on
N{s}ZN
is free the (locally defined) conjugation action of
M_{-}
on
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is (locally) free as well.

Now observe that by Proposition
3.4.3

\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}\subset N{s}ZN
. Since by Theorem
1.3.1
the conjugation action of
N
on
N{s}ZN
is free and regular,
sZN_{s}
being a cross–section for this action, and
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is closed, the induced action of
M_{-}\subset N
on
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is globally defined and is free as well. Therefore the quotient
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})
is a smooth variety.

Next observe that the definitions of
M_{-}^{s}
and of
N_{s}^{\prime}
and Lemma
3.4.4
imply the following factorization
N_{s}=M_{-}^{s}N_{s}^{\prime}
. Thus if
szn_{s}\in sZN_{s}
,
z\in Z
,
n_{s}\in N_{s}
then
n_{s}
can be uniquely factorized as
n_{s}=m_{s}n_{s}^{\prime}
,
m_{s}\in M_{-}^{s}
,
n_{s}^{\prime}\in N_{s}^{\prime}
and we have

szn_{s}=szm_{s}n_{s}^{\prime}.

Conjugating this element by
n_{s}^{\prime}
we deduce that
szn_{s}
is uniquely conjugated to the element

n_{s}^{\prime}szm_{s}\in N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}

and hence
N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}\simeq sZN_{s}=\Sigma_{s}
is a cross–section for the action of
N
on
NsZN
as well.

We show now that the closure of
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
contains the varieties
N_{-}sZM_{-}
and
N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}
.
Recall that by (
3.4.11
)
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))\subset N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}=N_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}sM_{-}
.
Observe that the proof of presentation (
3.4.26
), formula (
3.4.27
) and the definition of
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
imply that it contains elements of the form
ksn
for some
n\in M_{-}\subset N
and arbitrary
k\in N_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}
. This also follows from the fact that
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is closed with respect to the right multiplication by arbitrary elements from
Z_{+}
and with respect to the left multiplication by arbitrary elements from
N_{-}
, as
Z_{+}\subset N_{\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}}
, and
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is closed with respect to the right multiplication by arbitrary elements from
N_{\Delta_{+}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}}
, and
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is also closed with respect to the restriction of action (
3.4.27
) to
H_{0}
.

Now recall that
\bar{h}_{0}(\alpha)>0
for
\alpha\in\Delta_{+}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}
and
\bar{h}_{0}(\alpha)=0
for
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}
, and hence the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
–action on
G
induced by conjugations by the elements
h(t)
from the one–parameter subgroup generated by
-\bar{h}_{0}\in\mathfrak{h}
is contracting on
N
and fixes all elements of
Z
. Applying action (
3.4.27
) with
h=h(t)
to the elements
ksn
with arbitrary
k\in N_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}
we immediately deduce, with the help of (
3.4.27
), that the
M_{-}
–component
n
can be contracted to the identity element using the above defined contracting action, and the closure of
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
contains the variety
N_{-}Zs
as the closure of
Z_{-}H_{0}Z_{+}
is
Z
.

By definition
q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
is closed with respect to the left multiplication by arbitrary elements from
N_{-}
. Recall also that
M_{-}\subset N
freely acts on
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
by conjugations. Therefore
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
also contains the variety
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=N_{-}sZM_{-}
. In particular,
N_{s}^{\prime}sZM_{-}^{s}\subset\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
.

Note that

N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}\subset N_{-}ZsM_{-}\subset\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}\subset\overline{N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}}.

This implies after taking closures that

\overline{N_{-}ZsM_{-}}=\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}=\overline{N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}}.

But the variety
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
is closed. Indeed, let
N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}=N_{s^{-1}}\cap N_{-}
,
N^{\prime}=\{n\in N:s^{-1}ns\in N\}
. These definitions and Lemma
3.4.4
imply that
N\cap N_{-}=N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}N^{\prime}
. Note that
s
normalizes
Z
and
Z
normalizes
N^{\prime}
. Observe also that
s^{-1}N^{\prime}sM_{-}=N_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}
, where
N_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}
is the subgroup of
N
generated by one–parameter subgroups corresponding to the roots
\alpha\in\Delta_{-}^{s}
from the segment defined by the condition
\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}
.
Therefore every element
nzsm\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}=(N\cap N_{-})ZsM_{-}
,
n\in N\cap N_{-}
,
z\in Z
,
m\in M_{-}
with
n=n_{1}n_{2}
,
n_{1}\in N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}
,
n_{2}\in N^{\prime}
can be represented as follows

nzsm=n_{1}n_{2}zsm=n_{1}sz^{\prime}s^{-1}n_{2}^{\prime}sm\in N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}sZN_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}},z^{\prime}\in Z,n_{2}^{\prime}\in N^{\prime},

and any element of
N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}sZN_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}
can be obtained this way. Thus
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=(N\cap N_{-})ZsM_{-}=N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}sZN_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}
.

But the variety
\overline{N}ZN
is closed by Corollary
1.3.2
and

s^{-1}N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}sZN_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}\subset\overline{N}ZN

is a closed subvariety of it as
s^{-1}N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}s\subset\overline{N}
,
N_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}\subset N
are closed subgroups. Therefore
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=(N\cap N_{-})ZsM_{-}=N_{s^{-1}}^{\prime}sZN_{\alpha\leq-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}
is closed.

Hence

N_{-}ZsM_{-}=\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}=\overline{N_{-}sH_{0}Z_{+}M_{-}}.

Using Lemma
1.3.1
one can also easily obtain that
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
.

Finally we show that
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
is a cross–section for the free conjugation action of
M_{-}
on
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
.
Observe that any two points of
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
are not
M_{-}
–conjugate. Indeed, we have an inclusion
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}\subset\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
, and two points of
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
can not be
M_{-}
-conjugate if they are not
N
–conjugate in
N{s}ZN
. But
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
is a cross–section for the action of
N
on
ZsZN
. Thus any two points of
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
are not
N
–conjugate, and hence they are not
M_{-}
–conjugate.
Therefore the closed variety
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})
must contain the closed variety
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}\simeq\Sigma_{s}
.

From formula (
1.6.5
) for the cardinality
\sharp\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}
of the set
\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}
and from the definitions of
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
and of
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
we deduce that the dimension of the quotient
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})
is equal to the dimension of the variety
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
,

\displaystyle{\rm dim}~\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})={\rm dim}~G-2{\rm dim}~M_{-}=2D+l-2\sharp\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}=2D+l-2\left(D-\frac{l(s)-l^{\prime}}{2}-D_{0}\right)=

\displaystyle=l(s)+2D_{0}+l-l^{\prime}={\rm dim}~N_{s}+{\rm dim}~Z={\rm dim}~sZN_{s}={\rm dim}~\Sigma_{s}={\rm dim}~N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}.

Since
\pi_{q}
is a morphism of varieties
\pi_{q}^{-1}(N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s})=\pi_{q}^{-1}(\Sigma_{s})
is a closed smooth subvariety of the smooth variety
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
. The number of connected components of
\pi_{q}^{-1}(N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s})
is equal to the cardinality of the finite set
Z/Z^{0}
, where
Z^{0}
is the identity component of
Z
. Each such component is irreducible.

The number of connected components of the smooth closed variety
N_{-}ZsM_{-}=\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is also equal to the cardinality of the finite set
Z/Z^{0}
. Each such component is irreducible. The last two observations together with the identity for the dimensions imply
\pi_{q}^{-1}(N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s})=N_{-}ZsM_{-}
.

Therefore
\pi_{q}(\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))})\simeq N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
,
N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}
is a cross–section for the action of
M_{-}
on
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
, and the conjugation action

M_{-}\times N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}\rightarrow N_{-}ZsM_{-}

is an isomorphism of varieties. We conclude that the algebra
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{C}}[M_{-}]\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}]
,
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\cong{\mathbb{C}}[M_{-}]\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[N_{s}^{\prime}ZsM_{-}^{s}]
.

Now recall that by Lemma
3.4.1

W^{s}(G)={\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\cap C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{C^{\infty}(M_{+})},

where
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]
is regarded as a subalgebra in
C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
using the map
q^{*}:C^{\infty}(q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u)))\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
and the imbedding
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\subset C^{\infty}(q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u)))
.

By Lemma
3.4.2
the algebra
C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{M_{-}}
is isomorphic to
C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{C^{\infty}(M_{+})}
, and hence

W^{s}(G)={\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\cap C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{C^{\infty}(M_{+})}={\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\cap C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{M_{-}}.

(3.4.30)

As we already proved the variety
\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}
is stable under the conjugation action of
M_{-}
, and the map
\pi_{q}:\overline{q(\mu_{M_{+}}^{-1}(u))}\rightarrow\pi_{q}\overline{q(\mu_{M_{+}}^{-1}(u))}

is a morphism of varieties. Moreover, under the map
q:G^{*}\to G
the local dressing action of
M_{-}
on
G^{*}
becomes the conjugation action on
G
. Therefore the map

\mathbb{C}[\pi_{q}\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\cap C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))^{M_{-}},~~\psi\mapsto\pi_{q}^{*}\psi

(3.4.31)

is an isomorphism, where
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]
is regarded as a subalgebra in
C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))
using the map

q^{*}:C^{\infty}(q(\mu_{M_{+}}^{-1}(u)))\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))

and the imbedding
{\mathbb{C}}[\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]\subset C^{\infty}(q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u)))
.

Combining (
3.4.30
) and (
3.4.31
) we obtain that
W^{s}(G)\cong\mathbb{C}[\pi_{q}\overline{q(\mu_{M_{-}}^{-1}(u))}]
.
This completes the proof.

∎

3.5
Zhelobenko type operators for Poisson q-W–algebras

In this section we present the main result of this chapter, a formula for a projection operator
\Pi:\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]^{M_{-}}
onto the subspace of invariants
\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]^{M_{-}}
which is isomorphic to
W^{s}(G)
as an algebra according to Theorem
3.4.5
. This formula has a direct quantum analogue which will be introduced in the next chapter.

The operator
\Pi
can be defined following the philosophy of
[
109
]
where a similar projection operator onto the subspace
\mathbb{C}[NZsN]^{N}\subset\mathbb{C}[NZsN]
was defined and studied.
More precisely, according to Theorem
3.4.5
any
g\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}
can be uniquely represented in the form

g=nn_{s}zsm_{s}n^{-1},n\in M_{-},n_{s}\in N_{s}^{\prime},m_{s}\in M_{-}^{s},z\in Z.

(3.5.1)

If for
f\in\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]
we define
\Pi f\in\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]
by

(\Pi f)(g)=f(n^{-1}gn)=f(n_{s}zsm_{s})

(3.5.2)

then
\Pi f
is an
M_{-}
–invariant function, and any
M_{-}
–invariant regular function on
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
can be obtained this way. Moreover, by the definition
\Pi^{2}=\Pi
, i.e.
\Pi
is a projection onto
\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]^{M_{-}}
.

To obtain an explicit formula for the operator
\Pi
we firstly find an explicit formula for
n
in terms of
g
in (
3.5.1
). Denote by
\omega
the Chevalley anti–involution on
\mathfrak{g}
which is induced by the antiautomorphism
\omega
of
U_{h}^{s}(\mathfrak{g})
on
U(\mathfrak{g})\simeq U_{h}^{s}(\mathfrak{g})/hU_{h}^{s}(\mathfrak{g})
. We also denote the corresponding anti–involution of
G
by the same letter. The following proposition is an analogue of Proposition 2.11 in
[
109
]
, where a similar statement was proved for the action of
N
on
NZsN
.

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let
g=nn_{s}zsm_{s}n^{-1}\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}
. Let
\alpha_{i}
be the simple roots of a system of positive roots
\Delta_{+}
associated to
s
,
s_{i}
the corresponding simple reflections,
\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{D}
,
\beta_{j}=s_{i_{1}}\ldots s_{i_{j-1}}\alpha_{i_{j}}
a normal ordering of
\Delta_{+}
corresponding to
s
. Let
\omega_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots l
be the fundamental weights corresponding to
\Delta_{+}
,
v_{\omega_{i}}
a non–zero highest weight vector in the irreducible highest weight
G
–module
V_{\omega_{i}}
of highest weight
\omega_{i}
,
(\cdot,\cdot)
the contravariant non–degenerate bilinear form on
V_{\omega_{i}}
such that
(v,xw)=(\omega(x)v,w)
for any
v,w\in V_{\omega_{i}}
,
x\in\mathfrak{g}
and
(v_{\omega_{i}},v_{\omega_{i}})=1
. Then
n
can be uniquely factorized as
n=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})
and the numbers
t_{i}
can be found inductively by the following formula

t_{p}=c_{p}\frac{(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})}{(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})},

(3.5.3)

where
w_{p}=s_{\beta_{p}}\ldots s_{\beta_{1}}
,
w_{p-1}=s_{\beta_{p-1}}\ldots s_{\beta_{1}}
,
c_{p}
is a non–zero constant only depending on the choice of the representative
s_{\beta_{p}}\in G
and on the choice of the root vector
X_{-\beta_{p}}\in\mathfrak{g}
,
g_{p}=n_{p}^{-1}gn_{p}
,
n_{p}=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p-1}}(t_{p-1})
and it is assumed that
n_{1}=1
,
w_{0}=1
.

Proof.

The numbers
t_{p}
can be found by induction starting with
p=1
. We shall establish the induction step. The case
p=1
corresponding to the base of the induction can be considered in a similar way.

Assume that
t_{1},\ldots,t_{p-1}
have already been found. Then

\displaystyle g_{p}s^{-1}=n_{p}^{-1}gn_{p}s^{-1}=X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})n_{s}zsm_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})s^{-1},

where
n_{p}=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p-1}}(t_{p-1})
.
By Lemma
3.4.4
we can write

X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})n_{s}=m_{1}X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p}),m_{1}\in N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}.

Since
\Delta_{w_{p}^{-1}}=\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\}
we have
w_{p}^{-1}N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}w_{p}\subset N_{-}
, and hence

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},m_{1}X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})

as
v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector.

Now observe that
m_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})\in M_{-}^{s}N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{c}]}
. Note that the properties of the normal ordering in
\Delta_{+}
associated to
s
imply that
M_{-}^{s}=N_{[-\beta_{k},-\beta_{c}]}
for some
k
and that the union
[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{c}]\cup[-\beta_{k},-\beta_{c}]
is a minimal segment, so the subgroup
M_{-}^{s}N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{c}]}
is generated by one–parameter subgroups corresponding to the roots from that segment.
Thus using Lemma
3.4.4
one can uniquely factorize the element
m_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})
as

m_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})=m_{2}m_{3},m_{3}\in N_{[-\beta_{k},-\beta_{c}]},m_{2}\in N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{k-1}]},

where it is assumed that
N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{k-1}]}=1
if
p>k-1
. If
\alpha\in[\beta_{p},\beta_{k-1}]
then
s\alpha\in\Delta_{+}^{s}
and by the properties of the normal ordering (
1.6.3
) in Proposition
1.6.1
we have
s\alpha>\alpha
, and if
s\alpha+\alpha_{0}\in\Delta_{+}^{s}
for
\alpha_{0}\in\Delta_{0}
then
s\alpha+\alpha_{0}>\alpha
. Observing also that
Z
is generated by one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots from
\Delta_{0}
and by the centralizer of
s
in
H
which normalizes all one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots, we deduce
zsm_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p})s^{-1}=m_{4}zsm_{3}s^{-1}
,

m_{4}=zsm_{2}s^{-1}z^{-1}\in N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}
.

Now using Lemma
3.4.4
we can uniquely factorize the element
X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})m_{4}\in N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}
as
X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})m_{4}=m_{5}X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})
,

m_{5}\in N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}
. Remark that
w_{p}^{-1}N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}w_{p}\subset N_{-}
as
\Delta_{w_{p}^{-1}}=\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\}
and hence

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})m_{4}zsm_{3}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},m_{5}X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{3}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{3}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})

as
v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector.

By the definition of
m_{3}
we have
sm_{3}s^{-1}\in\overline{N}
. Denote by
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{D}
the normal ordering (
1.6.3
) in
\Delta_{+}^{s}
corresponding to
s
, so that
\beta_{p}=\delta_{q}
for some
q
. Applying arguments similar to those used above we can factorize
sm_{3}s^{-1}=m_{6}m_{7}
,
m_{6}\in N_{[\delta_{1},\delta_{q-1}]}
,
m_{7}\in N_{[\delta_{q},\delta_{D}]}
. Note that
[\delta_{q},\delta_{D}]\subset[\beta_{p},\beta_{D}]
, and each root from this segment remains positive under the action of
w_{p-1}^{-1}
. Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{3}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zm_{6}m_{7}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}),

where we assume that the root vectors
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
are chosen in such a way that
\omega(X_{-\beta_{p}})=X_{\beta_{p}}
.

Since
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{w_{p-1}^{-1}}
we infer
X_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=0
, and hence
w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector for the
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–triple generated by the elements
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
. Moreover, since
w_{p-1}^{-1}(-\beta_{p})=-\alpha_{i_{p}}
the vector
X_{-\alpha_{i_{p}}}=w_{p-1}^{-1}X_{-\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}
is a root vector corresponding to
-\alpha_{i_{p}}
, and hence
X_{-\beta_{p}}^{2}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=w_{p-1}X_{-\alpha_{i_{p}}}^{2}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=0
by the definition of
v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
. Therefore
w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector for the two–dimensional irreducible representation of the
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–triple generated by the elements
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
, and
s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a non–zero lowest weight vector for that representation. Recalling the standard
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–representation theory, the fact that each weight space is an eigenspace for the action of
H
and that
X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
we deduce

\displaystyle X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}+t_{p}X_{\beta_{p}}s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=

\displaystyle=s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}+\frac{t_{p}}{c_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},

where
c_{p}
is a non–zero constant only depending on the choice of the representative
s_{\beta_{p}}\in G
and on the choice of the root vectors
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
. The first term in the last sum has weight
-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
, and the second one
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
.

The vector
zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a linear combination of vectors of weights of the form
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0}
, where
\omega_{0}\in\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{*}
,
c_{r}\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
.
Since weight spaces corresponding to different weights are orthogonal with respect to the contravariant bilinear non–degenerate form on
V_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
the only nontrivial contributions to the product
(X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})
come from the products of vectors of weights either
-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
or
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
.

In the first case we must have
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0}=-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
, and hence
\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0}=-\delta_{q}
. In particular,
\overline{h}_{0}(\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0})=\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{r})=-\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{q})
which is impossible as
-\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{q})<0
and
c_{r}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{r})\geq 0
.

In the second case we must have
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0}=w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
or
\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0}=0
. In particular,
\overline{h}_{0}(\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\delta_{r}+\omega_{0})=\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}c_{r}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{r})=0
which forces
c_{r}=0
for all
r
as
\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{r})>0
and
c_{r}\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
, and hence
\omega_{0}=0
as well.

We conclude that the only nontrivial contributions to the product

(X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})

come from the products of vectors of weights
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
. By the above considerations only terms of the form
zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
may give contributions of weight
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
in the weight decomposition of the element
zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
, and this yields

\begin{array}[]{l}(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zm_{6}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=\\
\\
=\frac{t_{p}}{c_{p}}(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}).\end{array}

Therefore

t_{p}=c_{p}\frac{(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})}{(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})}.

Similar arguments show that

(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}).

Combining the last two identities we obtain formula (
3.5.3
).

∎

Formulas (
3.5.3
) have no direct quantum analogues, and for the purposes of quantization we shall need other formulas for the coefficients
t_{p}
introduced in the previous proposition. These formulas express the coefficients
t_{p}
in terms of other matrix elements of finite-dimensional irreducible representations of
G
. These matrix elements can be defined by specializing the results of Lemma 2.3 in
[
28
]
at
q=1
. By this lemma there are integral dominant weights
\mu_{p}(\mu_{p}^{\prime})
,
p=1,\ldots,D
and elements
v_{p}\in V_{\mu_{p}}
(
v_{p}^{\prime}\in V_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}}
) such that

(v_{p},X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}v_{\mu_{p}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&{\rm if}~X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}=X_{-\beta_{p}}\\
0&{\rm otherwise}\end{array}\right.,

(3.5.4)

(v_{p}^{\prime},X_{-\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&{\rm if}~X_{-\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}=X_{-\beta_{p}}\\
0&{\rm otherwise}\end{array}\right.,

where for
\alpha\in\Delta
,
k\in\mathbb{N}

X_{\alpha}^{(k)}=\frac{X_{\alpha}^{k}}{k!}
.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

Let
g=nn_{s}zsm_{s}n^{-1}\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}
be an element of
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
represented as in (
3.5.1
), where
n=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})
,
g_{p}=n_{p}^{-1}gn_{p}
,
n_{p}=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p-1}}(t_{p-1})
. Then
g_{p}\in N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}ZsM_{-}^{s}
,

g_{p}=X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D})zsm_{s}^{\prime},m_{s}^{\prime}\in M_{-}^{s},

(3.5.5)

r_{p}=t_{p},

and the numbers
t_{p}
can be found inductively by the following formula

t_{p}=\frac{(v_{p},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}})}{(v_{\mu_{p}},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}})}=\frac{(v_{p}^{\prime},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})}{(v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})}.

(3.5.6)

Proof.

We shall prove this proposition by induction. In the proof we shall use the notation and arguments from the proof of the previous proposition. Firstly we show that
g\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
. Indeed, using Lemma
3.4.4
we can represent any element
m\in M_{-}
as
m=m_{1}m_{2}
,
m_{1}\in N_{[-\beta_{1},-\beta_{k-1}]},m_{2}\in M_{-}^{s}
. Next, if
\alpha\in[\beta_{1},\beta_{k-1}]
then
s\alpha\in\Delta_{+}^{s}
and by the properties of the normal ordering (
1.6.3
) in Proposition
1.6.1
we have
s\alpha>\alpha
, and if
s\alpha+\alpha_{0}\in\Delta_{+}^{s}
for
\alpha_{0}\in\Delta_{0}
then
s\alpha+\alpha_{0}>\alpha
, and in both cases
s\alpha,s\alpha+\alpha_{0}\in\Delta_{+}
. Observing also that
Z
is generated by the one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots from
\Delta_{0}
and by the centralizer of
s
in
H
which normalizes all one–parameter subgroups corresponding to roots, we deduce
zsm_{1}s^{-1}\in N_{-}Z
for any
z\in Z
. Therefore
zsm=zsm_{1}s^{-1}sm_{2}\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
, so
ZsM_{-}\subset N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
, and hence
g\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}\subset N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
.

Now we shall prove the first two statements by induction.
Assume that

\displaystyle g_{p}=X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D})zsm_{s}^{\prime},z\in Z,m_{s}^{\prime}\in M_{-}^{s}.

(3.5.7)

Note that in the case when
p=1
this expression is already established as it directly follows from the fact that
g\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}^{s}
proved above.

We show first that
r_{p}=t_{p}
.
By Lemma
3.4.4
we can write

X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D})=m_{1}X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p}),m_{1}\in N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}.

Since
\Delta_{w_{p}^{-1}}=\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\}
we have
w_{p}^{-1}N_{[-\beta_{p+1},-\beta_{D}]}w_{p}\subset N_{-}
, and hence

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},m_{1}X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})zsm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})zsm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})

as
v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector.

Now recall that
m_{s}^{\prime}\in M_{-}^{s}
. Note that the properties of the normal ordering in
\Delta_{+}
associated to
s
imply that
M_{-}^{s}=N_{[-\beta_{k},-\beta_{c}]}
for some
k
.

By the definition of
m_{s}^{\prime}
we have
sm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}\in\overline{N}
. Denote by
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{D}
the normal ordering in
\Delta_{+}^{s}
corresponding to
s
, so that
\beta_{p}=\delta_{q}
for some
q
. Using arguments similar to those used above we can factorize
sm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}=mm^{\prime}
,
m\in N_{[\delta_{1},\delta_{q-1}]}
,
m^{\prime}\in N_{[\delta_{q},\delta_{D}]}
. Note that
[\delta_{q},\delta_{D}]\subset[\beta_{p},\beta_{D}]
, and each root from this segment remains positive under the action of
w_{p-1}^{-1}
. Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zsm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zmm^{\prime}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=

\displaystyle=(X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}),

where we assume that the root vectors
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
are chosen in such a way that
\omega(X_{-\beta_{p}})=X_{\beta_{p}}
.

Since
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{w_{p-1}^{-1}}
we infer
X_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=0
, and hence
w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector for the
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–triple generated by the elements
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
. Moreover, since
w_{p-1}^{-1}(-\beta_{p})=-\alpha_{i_{p}}
the vector
X_{-\alpha_{i_{p}}}=w_{p-1}^{-1}X_{-\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}
is a root vector corresponding to
-\alpha_{i_{p}}
, and hence
X_{-\beta_{p}}^{2}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=w_{p-1}X_{-\alpha_{i_{p}}}^{2}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=0
by the definition of
v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
. Therefore
w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a highest weight vector for the two–dimensional irreducible representation of the
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–triple generated by the elements
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
, and
s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a non–zero lowest weight vector for that representation. Recalling the standard
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
–representation theory, the fact that each weight space is an eigenspace for the action of
H
and that
X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=X_{\beta_{p}}(t_{p})s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
we deduce

\displaystyle X_{\beta_{p}}(r_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=X_{\beta_{p}}(r_{p})s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}+r_{p}X_{\beta_{p}}s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}=

\displaystyle=s_{\beta_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}+\frac{r_{p}}{c_{p}}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},

where
c_{p}
is a non–zero constant only depending on the choice of the representative
s_{\beta_{p}}\in G
and on the choice of the root vector
X_{\pm\beta_{p}}
. The first term in the last sum has weight
-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
, and the second one
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
.

The vector
zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
is a linear combination of vectors of weights of the form
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0}
, where
\omega_{0}\in\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{*}
,
c_{u}\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
.
Since weight spaces corresponding to different weights are orthogonal with respect to the contravariant bilinear non–degenerate form on
V_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
the only nontrivial contributions to the product
(X_{\beta_{p}}(r_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})
come from the products of vectors of weights either
-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
or
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
.

In the first case we must have
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0}=-\delta_{q}+w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
, and hence
\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0}=-\delta_{q}
. In particular,
\overline{h}_{0}(\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0})=\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{u})=-\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{q})
which is impossible as
-\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{q})<0
and
c_{u}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{u})\geq 0
.

In the second case we must have
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}+\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0}=w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
or
\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0}=0
. In particular,
\overline{h}_{0}(\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\delta_{u}+\omega_{0})=\sum_{u=1}^{q-1}c_{u}\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{u})=0
which forces
c_{u}=0
for all
u
as
\overline{h}_{0}(\delta_{u})>0
and
c_{u}\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
, and hence
\omega_{0}=0
as well.

We conclude that the only nontrivial contributions to the product

(X_{\beta_{p}}(r_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})

come from the products of vectors of weights
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
. By the above considerations only terms of the form
zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
may give contributions of weight
w_{p-1}\omega_{i_{p}}
in the weight decomposition of the element
zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}
, and this yields

\begin{array}[]{l}(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(X_{\beta_{p}}(r_{p})w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zmw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=\\
\\
=\frac{r_{p}}{c_{p}}(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}).\end{array}

Therefore

r_{p}=c_{p}\frac{(w_{p}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})}{(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})}.

Similar arguments show that

(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},zw_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}})=(w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}},g_{p}s^{-1}w_{p-1}v_{\omega_{i_{p}}}).

Combining the last two identities we obtain formula (
3.5.3
) for
r_{p}
, i. e.
t_{p}=r_{p}
. At the same time when
p=1
this yields formula (
3.5.3
) for
r_{1}
and establishes the base of induction.

g_{p+1}
is defined by putting
t_{p}=0
in the formula

g_{p}=X_{-\beta_{p}}(t_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})n_{s}zsm_{s}X_{-\beta_{c}}(-t_{c})\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}(-t_{p}),

(3.5.8)

and along with
t_{p}=r_{p}
(
3.5.7
) yields

g_{p+1}=X_{-\beta_{p+1}}(r_{p+1}^{\prime})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D}^{\prime})zsm_{s}^{\prime},z\in Z,m_{s}^{\prime}\in M_{-}^{s}.

This establishes the induction step, and hence the first two statements of this proposition are proved.

Now we establish the second expression for
t_{p}
in (
3.5.6
), the first one can be justified in a similar way.
By (
3.5.5
) we have

(v_{p}^{\prime},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=(v_{p}^{\prime},X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D})zsm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})

(3.5.9)

Recall that
sM_{-}^{s}s^{-1}\subset\overline{N}
by the definition of
M_{-}^{s}
. Note that
Z\overline{N}\subset G
is a Lie subgroup with the Lie algebra
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}+\mathfrak{z}
. Since
Z\overline{N}
is generated by the one–parameter subgroups corresponding to the roots from the set
\Delta_{+}^{s}\cup\Delta_{0}
and by
H_{0}
and the action of
G
on
V_{\mu_{p}}
is locally finite we can write
zsm_{s}^{\prime}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}}=xv_{\mu_{p}}
, where
x\in U(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}+\mathfrak{z})
.

Now observe that
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}+\mathfrak{z}=((\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{-})+\mathfrak{z}_{-})+\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\perp}+(\mathfrak{z}_{+}+(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{+}))
, where
\mathfrak{z}_{\pm}=z\cap\mathfrak{n}_{\pm}
and the terms
(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})+\mathfrak{z}_{\pm}
,
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\perp}
in the direct linear sum in the right hand side are Lie algebras. Therefore by the Poincaré-Birkhoff-Witt theorem
x
is a linear combination of terms of the form
x_{-}x_{0}x_{+}
,
x_{\pm}\in U((\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})+\mathfrak{z}_{\pm})

x_{0}\in U(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\perp})
. Since
v_{\mu_{p}}
is a highest weight vector only terms with
x_{+}\in\mathbb{C}
can contribute to the right hand side of (
3.5.9
). Rearranging scalar factors we can assume without loss of generality that for these terms
x_{+}=1
. Observe also that the roots
\alpha\in\Delta_{-}
corresponding to the root vectors generating the Lie algebra
(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{-})+\mathfrak{z}_{-}
satisfy the condition
\alpha>-\beta_{c}
and that
-\beta_{p}\leq-\beta_{c}
. Therefore (
3.5.4
) implies that only terms with
x_{-}\in\mathbb{C}
can contribute to the right hand side of (
3.5.9
). Rearranging scalar factors we can assume without loss of generality that for these terms
x_{-}=1
. Thus by (
3.5.4
) we deduce

(v_{p}^{\prime},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=(v_{p}^{\prime},X_{-\beta_{p}}(r_{p})\ldots X_{-\beta_{D}}(r_{D})x_{0}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=\mu_{p}^{\prime}(x_{0})r_{p}.

Similarly we obtain

(v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}},g_{p}s^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=(v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}},x_{0}v_{\mu_{p}^{\prime}})=\mu_{p}^{\prime}(x_{0}).

The last two identities imply the second expression in (
3.5.6
).

∎

Remark 3.5.3
.

Note that
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
is a closed subvariety in
G
. Therefore for each
p=1,\ldots,c
the right hand side of (
3.5.3
) is a regular function on
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
as the composition of the regular function

n=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})\mapsto t_{p}

defined on
M_{-}
and of isomorphism (
3.4.29
) of varieties. Hence the denominators in (
3.5.3
) and (
3.5.6
) must be canceled.

From Proposition
3.5.2
we immediately obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.5.4
.

Let
\phi_{k}
and
\psi_{k}
,
k=1,\ldots,D
be the regular functions on
G
defined as follows

\phi_{k}(g)=(v_{k},gs^{-1}v_{\mu_{k}}),k=1,\ldots,D,g\in G,

\psi_{k}(g)=(v_{k}^{\prime},gs^{-1}v_{\mu_{k}^{\prime}}),k=1,\ldots,D,g\in G.

Then the restrictions of the functions
\phi_{k},k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
{N_{-}sZM_{-}}={N_{-}sZM_{-}^{s}}
or the restrictions of the functions
\psi_{k}(g),k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
{N_{-}sZM_{-}}={N_{-}sZM_{-}^{s}}
generate the vanishing ideal of
{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZM_{-}^{s}}\subset{N_{-}sZM_{-}^{s}}
.

By repeating verbatim the arguments in the proof of Proposition
3.5.2
one can also obtain another corollary.

Corollary 3.5.5
.

The restrictions of the functions
\phi_{k},k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
or the restrictions of the functions
\psi_{k}(g),k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
generate the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}}\subset\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
.

Let
\Delta_{s}^{\prime}=(\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\cap\Delta_{s})\cup\{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}:\alpha>\gamma_{l^{\prime}}\}
,
N_{-}^{\prime}=N_{-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}}
.
Then the restrictions of the functions
\phi_{k}(g),k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
or the restrictions of the functions
\psi_{k}(g),k=1,\ldots,p-1
to
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
generate the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}\subset\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
.

Observing that in the notation of Proposition
3.5.2
for
g=nn_{s}zsm_{s}n^{-1}\in N_{-}ZsM_{-}
we have
g_{c+1}=n_{s}zsm_{s}=n_{c+1}^{-1}gn_{c+1}
,
n=n_{c+1}=X_{-\beta_{1}}(t_{1})\ldots X_{-\beta_{c}}(t_{c})
and recalling the definition of the operator
\Pi
in (
3.5.2
) we infer the following theorem from Proposition
3.5.2
.

Theorem 3.5.6
.

Let
G_{p}
,
p=1,\ldots,c
be the rational function on
G
defined by

G_{p}(g)=\frac{(v_{p},gs^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}})}{(v_{\mu_{p}},gs^{-1}v_{\mu_{p}})},

(3.5.10)

and
\Pi_{p}
the operator on the space of rational functions on
G
induced by conjugation by the element
\exp(-G_{p}X_{-\beta_{p}})
,

\Pi_{p}f(g)=f(\exp(-G_{p}(g)X_{-\beta_{p}})g\exp(G_{p}(g)X_{-\beta_{p}})).

(3.5.11)

Then the restriction of the composition
\Pi_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\Pi_{c}
to
\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]
is equal to the
projection operator
\Pi
onto the subspace
\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]^{M_{-}}
of
M_{-}
–invariant regular functions on
N_{-}ZsM_{-}
,
\Pi:\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[N_{-}ZsM_{-}]^{M_{-}}
,

\Pi=\Pi_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\Pi_{c}.

(3.5.12)

This theorem has a quantum counterpart which will be formulated and proved in the next chapter.
Corollary
3.5.5
and Theorem
3.5.6
are the only results of this chapter which will be used in Chapter
4
.

Bibliographic comments

The results on Poisson–Lie groups used in this book can be found in

and the relevant properties of classical r-matrices can be found in

,

can be found in

Q-W–algebras for realizations of quantum groups associated to Weyl group elements were introduced in
[
100
,
101

in the case of Coxeter elements and in
[
104
]
in the general situation. However, in the definitions given in those papers other forms of the quantum group are used. The definition of q-W–algebras in this book is more close to the one given in
[
106
]
; it uses the Ad locally finite part of the quantum group (see
[
55
]
, Chapter 7) which reduces to the algebra of regular functions on
G
when

. However, in this book we define all algebras over slightly different rings.

The exposition in Sections

and

follows
[

,
106
]
with some appropriate modifications.

The presentation of the results on Poisson reduction in Section
3.3
is close to
[
102
]
, Section 2.3. More details on the notion and the properties of dual pairs and Poisson reduction can be found in
[
95
]
, and for statements related to the moment map for Poisson–Lie group actions the reader is referred to
[
72
]
.

The original definition of the Poisson algebras
W^{s}(G)
using the classical Poisson reduction only was given in
[

]
.

The definition of the classical Zhelobenko type operator

in Section

is a modified version of the definition given in
