(u,(X_{\gamma_{1}}^{+})^{(m_{1})}\ldots(X_{\gamma_{n}}^{+})^{(m_{n})}({X}_{\gamma_{n+1}}^{+})^{(m_{n+1})}\ldots({X}_{\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}^{+})^{(m_{l^{\prime}})}v)=

(4.1.54)

=\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{D}}F_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{D}}(u,T_{\gamma_{1}}\ldots T_{\gamma_{n}}{T}_{\gamma_{n+1}}\ldots{T}_{\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}({X}^{-}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}})}\ldots({X}^{-}_{\beta_{1}})^{(n_{1})}(X^{+}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1})}\ldots(X^{+}_{\beta_{D}})^{(n_{D})}v),

where
F_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{D}}\in\mathbb{C}[q,q^{-1}]
. This proves the first formula in the statement of this lemma.

To justify the last two formulas in the statement of the lemma we observe that

{T}_{\gamma_{l^{\prime}}}\ldots{T}_{\gamma_{n+1}}T_{\gamma_{n}}\ldots T_{\gamma_{1}}
,
T_{s}^{-1}
and
\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}

act as the same transformations of
\mathfrak{h}\subset U_{h}(\mathfrak{h})
and apply Lemma
2.7.4
. This completes the proof.

∎

In the course of the proof of the previous lemma we obtained the following result.

Corollary 4.1.8
.

The products

({X}^{-}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}})}\ldots({X}^{-}_{\beta_{1}})^{(n_{1})}(X^{+}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1})}\ldots(X^{+}_{\beta_{D}})^{(n_{D})}

or

(X^{+}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1})}\ldots(X^{+}_{\beta_{D}})^{(n_{D})}({X}^{-}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}})}\ldots({X}^{-}_{\beta_{1}})^{(n_{1})}

form a
U_{q}^{res}(H)
–basis in the subalgebra
U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
in
U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{g})
generated over
U_{q}^{res}(H)
by the elements

({X}^{-}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}})},\ldots,({X}^{-}_{\beta_{1}})^{(n_{1})},(X^{+}_{\beta_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1}})^{(n_{k_{l^{\prime}}+1})},\ldots,(X^{+}_{\beta_{D}})^{(n_{D})},n_{i}\in\mathbb{N},i=1,\ldots,D.

4.2
Some auxiliary results on the quantized algebra of regular functions on an algebraic Poisson–Lie group

In this short section we give several formulas related to the adjoint action and commutation relations in the algebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Firstly, following
[
12
]
, Theorem I.8.16 we recall the commutation relations in the algebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
which follow from the fact that
U_{h}^{s}(\mathfrak{g})
is quasitriangular.
Namely, if
V
,
V^{\prime}
are finite rank representations of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
,
(V)_{\eta},(V^{\prime})_{\rho}
,
(V)_{\beta},(V^{\prime})_{\gamma}
their weight subspaces of weights
\eta,\rho,\beta
and
\gamma
, respectively, and
v\in(V)_{\eta},v_{1}\in(V^{\prime})_{\rho}
,
u\in(V)_{\beta},u_{1}\in(V^{\prime})_{\gamma}
then evaluating the identity
\Delta_{s}^{opp}(x)\mathcal{R}^{s}=\mathcal{R}^{s}\Delta_{s}(x)
on the matrix element
(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})
and recalling formula (
2.6.11
) we obtain

q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\eta^{\vee},\rho^{\vee})}\left((u_{1},\cdot v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot v)+\sum_{\nu,i}(u_{1},\cdot u_{\nu,i}v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot u_{-\nu,i}v)\right)=

(4.2.1)

=q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\beta^{\vee},\gamma^{\vee})}(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})+\sum_{\nu,i}q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)(\beta^{\vee}+\nu^{\vee}),\gamma^{\vee}-\nu^{\vee})}(\omega(u_{-\nu,i})u,\cdot v))\otimes(\omega(u_{\nu,i})u_{1},\cdot v_{1}),

where

u_{-\nu,i}=c_{-\nu,i}f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})},n_{1}\beta_{1}+\ldots+n_{D}\beta_{D}=\nu,

u_{\nu,i}=c_{\nu,i}e_{\beta_{1}}^{n_{1}}\ldots e_{\beta_{D}}^{n_{D}},n_{1}\beta_{1}+\ldots+n_{D}\beta_{D}=\nu,

c_{\pm\nu,i}\in\mathcal{B}
,
and similarly evaluating the identity
\Delta_{s}^{opp}(x){\mathcal{R}^{s}_{21}}^{-1}={\mathcal{R}^{s}_{21}}^{-1}\Delta_{s}(x)
on the matrix element
(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})
we get

q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\eta^{\vee},\rho^{\vee})}\left((u_{1},\cdot v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot v)+\sum_{\nu,i}(u_{1},\cdot u_{-\nu,i}^{\prime}v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot u_{\nu,i}^{\prime}v)\right)=

=q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\beta^{\vee},\gamma^{\vee})}(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})+\sum_{\nu,i}q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)(\beta^{\vee}+\nu^{\vee}),\gamma^{\vee}-\nu^{\vee})}(\omega(u_{\nu,i}^{\prime})u,\cdot v))\otimes(\omega(u_{-\nu,i}^{\prime})u_{1},\cdot v_{1}),

where

u_{-\nu,i}^{\prime}=c_{-\nu,i}^{\prime}f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})},n_{1}\beta_{1}+\ldots+n_{D}\beta_{D}=\nu,

u_{\nu,i}^{\prime}=c_{\nu,i}^{\prime}e_{\beta_{D}}^{n_{D}}\ldots e_{\beta_{1}}^{n_{1}},n_{1}\beta_{1}+\ldots+n_{D}\beta_{D}=\nu,

c_{\pm\nu,i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
.

If
v=T_{s^{-1}}v_{\lambda}\in(V)_{s^{-1}\lambda},v_{1}=T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu}\in(V^{\prime})_{s^{-1}\mu}
,
u\in(V)_{\beta},u_{1}=v_{\mu}\in(V^{\prime})_{\mu}
, where
v_{\lambda}\in V
and
v_{\mu}\in V^{\prime}
are highest weight vectors, then the previous identity yields

q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\lambda^{\vee},\mu^{\vee})}(v_{\mu},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\otimes(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\lambda})=

(4.2.2)

=q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\beta^{\vee},\mu^{\vee})}(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\lambda})\otimes(v_{\mu},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu}).

The next lemma shows how the adjoint action behaves with respect to the multiplication in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Lemma 4.2.1
.

For any
f,g\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G],x\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})
we have

{\rm Ad}_{0}x(f\otimes g)={\rm Ad}_{0}x^{2}f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(x^{1})\cdot\omega_{0}x^{3}),

where
\Delta_{s}^{2}x=(\Delta_{s}\otimes id)\Delta_{s}x=x^{1}\otimes x^{2}\otimes x^{3}
in the Sweedler notation.

In particular,

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}(f\otimes g)=f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(f_{\beta})\cdot)+{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}f\otimes g(G_{\beta}\cdot)+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}x_{i}f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(y_{i})\cdot)+

(4.2.3)

+f(G_{\beta}\cdot G_{\beta}^{-1})\otimes g(G_{\beta}\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}))+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}y_{i}^{2}f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(y_{i}^{1})\cdot\omega_{0}x_{i})=

=f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(f_{\beta})\cdot)+{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}f\otimes g(G_{\beta}\cdot)+f(G_{\beta}\cdot G_{\beta}^{-1})\otimes g(G_{\beta}\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}))+

+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}y_{i}f\otimes g(G_{\beta}\cdot\omega_{0}x_{i})+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}x_{i}^{1}f\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(y_{i})\cdot\omega_{0}x_{i}^{2})

and

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}(f\otimes g)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{p=0}^{n-k}q_{\beta}^{-k(n-k)-p(n-k-p)}{\rm Ad}_{0}(G_{\beta}^{-k}f_{\beta}^{(p)})(f)\otimes g(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(G_{\beta}^{-k-p}f_{\beta}^{(n-k-p)})\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)}))+

+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i}q_{\beta}^{-k(n-k)}{\rm Ad}_{0}(G_{\beta}^{-k}x_{i}^{(n-k)})(f)\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(G_{\beta}^{-k}y_{i}^{(n-k)})\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)}))+

(4.2.4)

+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}({y_{i}^{(n)}}^{2})(f)\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})({y_{i}^{(n)}}^{1})\cdot\omega_{0}(x_{i}^{(n)})),

where
G_{\beta}
,
x_{i}
,
y_{i}
,
x_{i}^{(p)}
,
y_{i}^{(p)}
are defined in (
2.7.12
) and (
2.7.13
), and

\Delta_{s}(x_{i})=x_{i}^{1}\otimes x_{i}^{2}
,
\Delta_{s}(y_{i})=y_{i}^{1}\otimes y_{i}^{2}
,
\Delta_{s}(y_{i}^{(p)})={y_{i}^{(p)}}^{1}\otimes{y_{i}^{(p)}}^{2}
in the Sweedler notation.

Proof.

Denote using the Sweedler notation

\Delta_{s}^{3}x=(\Delta_{s}\otimes id\otimes id)(\Delta_{s}\otimes id)\Delta_{s}x=x^{1}\otimes x^{2}\otimes x^{3}\otimes x^{4}

and observe that the definition of
{\rm Ad}^{\prime}
implies that for any
x,z\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})

\Delta_{s}^{opp}({\rm Ad}^{\prime}xz)=(x^{2}\otimes x^{1})(z^{2}\otimes z^{1})(S_{s}x^{3}\otimes S_{s}x^{4})={\rm Ad}^{\prime}x^{2}z^{2}\otimes x^{1}z^{1}S_{s}x^{3}.

Let
z=S_{s}\omega_{0}y
,
y\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})
. Then, since
\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}
is an algebra homomorphism and a coalgebra anti-homomorphism, we deduce

\Delta_{s}\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}({\rm Ad}^{\prime}xS_{s}\omega_{0}y)=(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}\otimes\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})\Delta_{s}^{opp}({\rm Ad}^{\prime}xz)=(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}\otimes\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}){\rm Ad}^{\prime}x^{2}z^{2}\otimes x^{1}z^{1}S_{s}x^{3}=

=(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}\otimes\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}){\rm Ad}^{\prime}x^{2}(S_{s}\omega_{0})(y^{1})\otimes x^{1}(S_{s}\omega_{0})(y^{2})S_{s}x^{3}=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}{\rm Ad}^{\prime}x^{2}((S_{s}\omega_{0})(y^{1}))\otimes(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(x^{1})y^{2}\omega_{0}x^{3}.

Evaluating the last identity on
f\otimes g
we get the first formula in the statement of the lemma. (
4.2.3
) and (
4.2.4
) are obtained from it using (
2.7.12
) and (
2.7.13
).

∎

4.3
Properties of the quantized vanishing ideal of the level surface of the moment map for q-W–algebras

In this section we study the properties of the quantum counterpart
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
of the vanishing ideal of the variety
\overline{N_{-}ZHsM_{-}^{s}}
, and the properties of a
\mathcal{B}
–submodule
J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
which is a quantum counterpart of the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}\supset\overline{N_{-}ZHsM_{-}^{s}}
.

We start with a technical lemma which will allow us to use properties of the vanishing ideals of the varieties
\overline{N_{-}ZHsM_{-}^{s}}
and
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
to prove some properties of their quantum counterparts
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

Let
X
be a free
\mathcal{B}
–module,
V\subset V^{\prime}\subset X
two its submodules such that
V=V^{\prime}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)V^{\prime}
. Let
V_{q}=V\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathbb{C}}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
V_{q}^{\prime}=V^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathbb{C}}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
. Then
V_{q}=V^{\prime}_{q}
.

Proof.

First observe that by Theorem 6.5 in
[
92
]

V^{\prime}\subset X
is
\mathcal{B}
–free, and
V\subset V^{\prime}
is a free submodule of
V^{\prime}
. Let
e_{a}
,
a\in A
be a basis of
V^{\prime}
. Then as shown in the proof of Theorem 6.5 in
[
92
]

V
has a basis elements of which has the form
f_{a}=\sum_{b\leq a}c_{a}^{b}e_{b}
,
a\in B\subset A
, where
c_{a}^{a}\neq 0
,
c_{a}^{b}\in\mathcal{B}
. Since
V=V^{\prime}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)V^{\prime}
we must have
B=A
, and
c_{a}^{a}\neq 0
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)
, i.e.
c_{a}^{a}=1+(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)h_{a}
,
h_{a}\in\mathcal{B}
.

Now using a simple transfinite induction we can express the elements of the basis
e_{a}
regarded as elements of
V^{\prime}_{q}
in terms of
f_{a}
. Indeed, if
a_{0}\in A
is the minimal element then
e_{a_{0}}={c_{a_{0}}^{a_{0}}}^{-1}f_{a_{0}}
, and assuming that
e_{d}=\sum_{b\leq d}g_{d}^{b}f_{b}
,
g_{d}^{b}\in{\mathbb{C}}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
g_{d}^{d}\neq 0
holds for all
d<a
we get
e_{a}={c_{a}^{a}}^{-1}(f_{a}-\sum_{b<a}c_{a}^{b}e_{b})={c_{a}^{a}}^{-1}(f_{a}-\sum_{b<a}c_{a}^{b}\sum_{h\leq b}g_{b}^{h}f_{h})
, where all sums are finite. This establishes the base of the induction and completes the proof.

∎

The following lemma describes
{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
as a vanishing ideal, in complete analogy with the classical situation.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
.

Let
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
be the subalgebra in
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by the elements
f_{\beta}^{(n)}
,
\beta\in\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\supset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are stable under the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
. Thus
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are naturally equipped with the
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
–action induced by the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Let
\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}=\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\cap\Delta_{s}^{s}
and
\Delta_{0}^{+}=\Delta_{0}\cap\Delta_{+}
. Both
\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s},\Delta_{0}^{+}\subset\Delta_{+}
are minimal segments. Denote by
U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
and
U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
the subalgebras of
U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{g})
corresponding to
-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}
and
\Delta_{0}^{+}
, respectively.
Let
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements vanishing on
xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
. Denote
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}={\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
. Then
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
and
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}
. Thus
{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]={\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Moreover,
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=\overline{J}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
\overline{J}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
.

Proof.

From formula (
4.2.4
) with
g(\cdot)=(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
, and arbitrary
f
we have, since
u
is a highest weight vector,

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}(f\otimes g)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{p=0}^{n-k}q_{\beta}^{-k(n-k)-p(n-k-p)}{\rm Ad}_{0}(G_{\beta}^{-k}f_{\beta}^{(p)})(f)\otimes g(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(G_{\beta}^{-k-p}f_{\beta}^{(n-k-p)})\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)}))+

+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i}q_{\beta}^{-k(n-k)}{\rm Ad}_{0}(G_{\beta}^{-k}x_{i}^{(n-k)})(f)\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})(G_{\beta}^{-k}y_{i}^{(n-k)})\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)}))+

(4.3.1)

+\sum_{i}{\rm Ad}_{0}({y_{i}^{(n)}}^{2})(f)\otimes g((\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1})({y_{i}^{(n)}}^{1})\cdot\omega_{0}(x_{i}^{(n)}))=

=\sum_{k=0}^{n}q_{\beta}^{-k(n-k)}{\rm Ad}_{0}(G_{\beta}^{-k}f_{\beta}^{(n-k)})(f)\otimes g(G_{\beta}^{n}\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)})).

Since
u
is a highest weight vector and
U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
is invariant under multiplication by
\omega_{0}((X^{-}_{\beta})^{(k)})
from the right,
f_{\beta}=q^{-K\beta^{\vee}}X^{-}_{\beta}
, and
q^{-K\beta^{\vee}}
normalizes
w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+})
, elements
g(G_{\beta}^{n}\cdot\omega_{0}(f_{\beta}^{(k)}))
have the same properties as
g
.
Now from the definition of
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
it follows that
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}(f\otimes g)\in{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
.
Since elements
f\otimes g
, with
f,g\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
as above, span
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
we deduce
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}({\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}})\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
.

If we naturally extend the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})
to
\mathbb{C}_{q}[G]
the last inclusion implies
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}({\overline{I}^{1}_{q}})\subset{\overline{I}^{1}_{q}}
, and hence
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}({\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}})\subset{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
as clearly
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G])\subset\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
and
{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}({\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}})={\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta}^{(n)}({\overline{I}^{1}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G])\subset{\overline{I}^{1}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]={\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
.

Now we justify the second claim of the lemma. Let
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
be such that
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
.
First note that if
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}
then
s\alpha\in\Delta_{+}
and if
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{+}
then
s\alpha=\alpha
. Therefore applying the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt theorem for
U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
as stated in Corollary
4.1.8
and Lemma
2.4.2
we obtain that for
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))

(u,T_{s}xv)=(u,T_{s}\sum_{i}z^{+}_{i}x^{\prime}_{i}v),z_{i}^{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}_{i}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}).

Since for all
i

z^{+}_{i}x^{\prime}_{i}\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
we deduce that
(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
if and only if

(u,T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
, and hence
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
coincides with the left ideal generated by the elements
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
such that
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
satisfies
(u,T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
.

Obviously
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

On the other hand
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\overline{J}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
\overline{J}
is the ideal in
\mathbb{C}[G]
generated by matrix elements of the form
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
U(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,sz_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+})=U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})/(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})=U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})/(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
. We claim that
\overline{J}
coincides with the ideal of regular functions vanishing on the closure of
N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}
.

The proof of this claim is based on the description of closed subvarieties in
B_{-}\setminus G
in terms of the so–called generalized Plücker coordinates.

Recall that matrix elements of the form
(u,\cdot v)
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
U(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
, can be regarded as sections of line bundles on
B_{-}\setminus G
(see
[
35
]
). They are also called generalized Plücker coordinates on
B_{-}\setminus G
.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

Any closed subvariety in
B_{-}\setminus G
is the zero locus of a finite set of generalized Plücker coordinates.

Proof.

Indeed, the flag variety
B_{-}\setminus G
can be realized as the
G
–orbit
\mathcal{O}
of the line
[u]
defined by a non–zero lowest weight vector
u
in the projectivisation
P(V^{*})
of a finite-dimensional irreducible representation
V^{*}
of
G
dual to a highest weight irreducible representation
V
with a regular dominant highest weight (see e.g.
[
9
]
, Section 2 or
[
43
]
, §4). If we identify
V
with
V^{*}
using the contravariant bilinear form then a highest weight vector
u
of
V
becomes a lowest weight vector in
V^{*}
and the class
[g]\in B_{-}\setminus G
of an element
g\in G
corresponds to
\omega(g)[u]=[\omega(g)u]\in\mathcal{O}\subset P(V^{*})
.

Now let
v\in V
. Then

(u,gv)=(\omega(g)u,v)=(x,v),x=\omega(g)u\in V^{*},

(4.3.2)

so that
[x]=[\omega(g)u]\in\mathcal{O}\subset P(V^{*})
.

Any
y\in V^{*}
can be written in the form
y=\sum_{n=1}^{S}c_{n}e_{n}\in V^{*}
, where
e_{n}
,
n=1,\ldots,S
is a weight basis of
V^{*}
. The functions
\phi_{n}(y)=c_{n}
are linear coordinates on
V^{*}
. Moreover, since
V\simeq V^{*}
one can find elements
v_{n}\in V
such that
\phi_{n}(y)=(y,v_{n})
. In particular,
\phi_{n}
generate the algebra of polynomial functions on
V^{*}
, and any closed subvariety in
P(V^{*})
, and hence in
\mathcal{O}
, is the zero locus of a finite collection of some polynomials homogeneous in
\phi_{n}
(see
[
47
]
, Ch. 1, §2).

If
f(\phi_{1},\ldots\phi_{S})
is such a polynomial of degree
d
then the equation

f(\phi_{1}(y),\ldots\phi_{S}(y))=0
is well–defined in
P(V^{*})
, i.e. if
y\in V^{*}
is its solution then any element of the line
[y]\in P(V^{*})
is also its solution. Now using (
4.3.2
), the definition of
\phi_{n}
and the homogeneity of
f
we deduce that for
[x]\in\mathcal{O}

f(\phi_{1}(x),\ldots\phi_{S}(x))=0
if and only if
f((u,gv_{1}),\ldots,(u,gv_{S}))=0
for any
g\in G
, where
[g]\in B_{-}\setminus G
corresponds to
[x]
under the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}\simeq B_{-}\setminus G
. Note also that using algebraic rules for matrix elements we immediately obtain that
f((u,\cdot v_{1}),\ldots,(u,\cdot v_{S}))
is a generalized Plücker coordinate defined using the representation
V^{\otimes d}
and the highest weight vector

u^{\otimes d}=\underbrace{u\otimes\ldots\otimes u}_{d~{\rm times}}\in V^{\otimes d},

i.e.
f((u,\cdot v_{1}),\ldots,(u,\cdot v_{S}))=(u^{\otimes d},\cdot v)
for some
v\in V^{\otimes d}
.
The last two facts imply that any closed subvariety in
B_{-}\setminus G
is the zero locus of a finite set of generalized Plücker coordinates.

∎

We proceed with the proof of Lemma
4.3.2
.
Let
Z_{\pm}=Z\cap N_{\pm}
. Then
Z_{-}HZ_{+}
is dense in
ZH
, and hence the closure of
N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}
coincides with the closure of
N_{-}sZ_{-}HZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
. Since
s
fixes
\Delta_{0}
we can also write

N_{-}sZ_{-}HZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}=B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
and

\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}=\overline{B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}}.

(4.3.3)

Since
B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
is the preimage of
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
with respect to the natural projection
G\rightarrow B_{-}\setminus G
,
(u,gv)=0
for any
g\in B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
if and only if
(u,\cdot v)
regarded as a Plücker coordinate vanishes on
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
. By Lemma
4.3.3
the closure of
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
is the zero locus of a finite set of generalized Plücker coordinates vanishing on
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
which is dense in its closure.
Hence the vanishing ideal of
\overline{B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}}
is generated by the matrix elements
(u,\cdot v)
which correspond to these Plücker coordinates.

If
(u,\cdot v)
is any generalized Plücker coordinate vanishing on
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
all its derivatives in the directions tangent to
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
must vanish as well. Therefore
(u,sz_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
and
z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+})
.

Conversely, any generalized Plücker coordinate of the form
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
U(\mathfrak{g})
and
v\in V
, obeying the last property gives rise to a generalized Plücker coordinate vanishing on
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
and hence on the closure of
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
.

Thus the closure of
B_{-}\setminus B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}
is the zero locus of the set of the generalized Plücker coordinates of the above described type, and by the results proved above the corresponding matrix elements also generate the vanishing ideal of
\overline{B_{-}sZ_{+}M_{-}^{s}}
. By (
4.3.3
) we deduce that this ideal also coincides with the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
. Hence the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
is generated by functions of the form
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}[G]
, where where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
U(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V

satisfies
(u,sz_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s})
and
z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+})
, i.e.
\overline{J}
coincides with the ideal of regular functions vanishing on the closure of
N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}
.

All derivatives of elements of
\overline{J}
in the directions tangent to
N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}
must vanish.
Therefore every element of
\overline{J}
must vanish on elements
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
for any
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
.

Conversely, any element of
\mathbb{C}[G]
vanishing on on elements
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
for any
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
must also vanish on the closure of
N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}
, and hence from the definition of
\overline{J}
we deduce that
\overline{J}
coincides with the ideal generated by the elements vanishing on
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
for arbitrary
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
.
Therefore
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}={\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\overline{J}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Note that
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
are submodules of the
\mathcal{B}
–module
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
which is free by Proposition
3.2.6
. Therefore by Lemma
4.3.1

{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}
. This completes the proof.

∎

The following lemma will be needed later to cancel some terms in formula (
4.2.4
) for the adjoint action.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

For any
u
from the augmentation ideal of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
,
u\not\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\Delta_{0}^{-})
we have
(v_{\mu},\cdot uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\in{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

It suffices to verify the statement for
u=f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\neq 1
,
u\not\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\Delta_{0}^{-})
. For any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
we have

(v_{\mu},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=0

if
x
is from the augmentation ideal of
U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. Thus without loss of generality we can assume that
x=1
. In this case

(v_{\mu},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=(v_{\mu},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=k(v_{\mu},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=

=k(v_{\mu},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots f_{\beta_{m}}^{(n_{m})}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu}),k\in\mathcal{B}.

(4.3.4)

Let
\{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{m}\}=\Delta_{+}\cap\Delta_{+}^{s}
. Since
s(\beta_{p})\in\Delta_{-}
for
p>m
, the right hand side of (
4.3.4
) vanishes if there exists
p>m
such that
n_{p}\neq 0
.

If
n_{p}=0
for all
p>m
then, assuming without loss of generality that
z_{+}
and
x^{\prime}
are monomials in quantum root vectors, we infer that the weight of the element
z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots f_{\beta_{m}}^{(n_{m})}
is not zero as
(\overline{h}_{0},wt(z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\ldots f_{\beta_{m}}^{(n_{m})}))<0
. Hence the right hand side of (
4.3.4
) vanishes in this case as well.

Thus
(v_{\mu},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=0
in all cases and by the definition of
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
given in Lemma
4.3.2

(v_{\mu},\cdot uT_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\in{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

∎

Next, as it was promised in the beginning of this section, we define a
\mathcal{B}
–submodule
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
which is a quantum counterpart of the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}\supset\overline{N_{-}ZHsM_{-}^{s}}
. The next three lemmas are similar to Lemmas
4.3.2
and
4.3.4
for
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
.

Lemma 4.3.5
.

Let
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. Denote
J^{1}_{q}={J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
and
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=J^{1}_{q}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. Then
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are two sided ideals in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
stable under the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
. Thus
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are naturally equipped with the
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
–action induced by the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Proof.

From commutation relations (
4.2.1
) it follows that
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is in fact a two sided ideal. First without loss of generality we can assume that
v
belongs to a weight subspace. Indeed, for any given
V
and
u\in V
as in the statement of this lemma
U_{q}(H)
naturally acts on the subspace of
V
spanned by elements
v\in V
such that
(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. Therefore this subspace is
P
–graded.

From (
4.2.1
) with
u,v
as in the statement of the lemma and arbitrary
(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
such that
v\in(V)_{\eta},v_{1}\in(V^{\prime})_{\rho}
,
u\in(V)_{\beta},u_{1}\in(V^{\prime})_{\gamma}
we have

q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}+id)\eta^{\vee},\rho^{\vee})}\left((u_{1},\cdot v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot v)+\sum_{\nu,i}(u_{1},\cdot u_{\nu,i}v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot u_{-\nu,i}v)\right)=

(4.3.5)

=q^{((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}+id)\beta^{\vee},\gamma^{\vee})}(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})

since
u
is a highest weight vector. Observe that
U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
is invariant with respect to the right multiplication by the elements
u_{-\nu,i}
, and hence
(u,\cdot u_{-\nu,i}v)
vanishes on elements of the form
T_{s}x
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. We deduce that
(u_{1},\cdot u_{\nu,i}v_{1})\otimes(u,\cdot u_{-\nu,i}v)\in{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, and hence (
4.3.5
) implies
(u,\cdot v)\otimes(u_{1},\cdot v_{1})\in{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, i.e.
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a right ideal as well.

This immediately implies that
J^{1}_{q}
is a two sided ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{q}[G]
, and hence
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=J^{1}_{q}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
is a two sided ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Similarly to the proof of an analogous property for
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
in Lemma
4.3.2
(see formula (
4.3.1
)) one can deduce that
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are stable with respect to the
{\rm Ad}_{0}
-action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
.

∎

Similarly to Lemma
4.3.2
one can describe
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
as a vanishing ideal.

Lemma 4.3.6
.

Let
\Delta_{s}^{\prime}=(\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\cap\Delta_{s}^{s})\cup\{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}:\alpha>\gamma_{l^{\prime}}\}
and
\Delta_{0}^{+}=\Delta_{0}\cap\Delta_{+}
. Both
\Delta_{s}^{\prime},\Delta_{0}^{+}\subset\Delta_{+}
are minimal segments. Denote by
U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
and
U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
the subalgebras of
U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{g})
corresponding to
-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}
and
\Delta_{0}^{+}
, respectively.
Let
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements
vanishing on
xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
. Denote
{J^{1}_{q}}={J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{J^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}={J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
. Then
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
and
{J^{1}_{q}}={J^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}
. In particular,
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{1}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]={J^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Moreover,
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=J
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
J
is the vanishing ideal of the variety
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Let
u
be a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
,
v\in V
.

First note that if
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\setminus\Delta_{s}^{\prime}
then
s\alpha\in\Delta_{+}
and if
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{+}
then
s\alpha=\alpha
. Therefore applying the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt theorem for
U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
as stated in Corollary
4.1.8
and Lemma
2.4.2
we obtain that for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})

(u,T_{s}xv)=(u,T_{s}\sum_{i}z_{i}^{+}x^{\prime}v),z_{i}^{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}_{i}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}).

Since for all
i

z^{+}_{i}x^{\prime}_{i}\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
we deduce that

(u,T_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
if and only if

(u,T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
,
z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
, and hence
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
coincides with the left ideal generated by the elements
(u,\cdot v)
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
,
v\in V
is such that
(u,T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
.

Obviously
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

On the other hand
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=J
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
J
is the ideal in
\mathbb{C}[G]
generated by matrix elements of the form
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
U(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,sz_{+}x^{\prime}v)=0
for any
z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+})=U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})/(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})=U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})/(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
. Similarly to the proof of the second claim of Lemma
4.3.2
one can show that
J
coincides with the ideal of regular functions vanishing on
\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}}=\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}=\overline{B_{-}sZ_{+}N_{-}^{\prime}}
.

All derivatives of elements of
{J}
in the directions tangent to
N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}
must vanish. Therefore every element of
J
must vanish on elements
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
. Conversely, if
(u^{\prime},\cdot v^{\prime})
is a regular function such that
(u^{\prime},xshz_{+}x^{\prime}v^{\prime})=0
for any
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
,
h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
,
x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
and
z_{-}\in U(-\Delta_{0}^{+})
then
(u^{\prime},\cdot v^{\prime})
vanishes on
N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}
, and hence on its closure. Thus
J
coincides with the ideal of regular functions vanishing on elements
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x\in U(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
.
Therefore
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=J
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Recall that
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=J
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.
Note also that
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
are submodules of the
\mathcal{B}
–module
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
which is free by Proposition
3.2.6
. Therefore by Lemma
4.3.1

{J^{1}_{q}}={J^{1}_{q}}^{\prime}
. This completes the proof.

∎

The following lemma will be needed later to cancel some terms in formula (
4.2.4
) for the adjoint action.

Lemma 4.3.7
.

For any
\beta\in\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}\setminus\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and
n>0
we have
(v_{\mu},\cdot f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\in{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

For any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
we have

(v_{\mu},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=0

if
x
is from the augmentation ideal of
U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. Thus without loss of generality we can assume that
x=1
. We can also assume without loss of generality that
z_{+}
and
x^{\prime}
are monomials in quantum root vectors. In the proof of Lemma
4.4.3
we shall consider a more general situation and show that in this case the weight of the element
T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}
is not zero (see the arguments after formula (
4.4.7
)). Thus

(v_{\mu},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=(v_{\mu},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=k(v_{\mu},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})=0,

where
k\in\mathcal{B}
, and hence by Lemma
4.3.6

(v_{\mu},\cdot f_{\beta}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\in{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

∎

4.4
Higher quantized vanishing ideals

The next step in our approach is, as it was mentioned in the introduction to this chapter, the definition and the study of quantum analogues
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
of the vanishing ideals of the varieties

\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}}\subset\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}},\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}\subset\overline{N_{-}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}.

Recall that according to Corollary
3.5.5
the vanishing ideals of these varieties are defined in terms of functions
\phi_{k}
introduced in Corollary
3.5.4
.
Therefore firstly we have to define quantum analogues
A_{p}
of these functions. This is done with the help of quantum counterparts of matrix elements (
3.5.4
). More precisely, by Proposition 8.3 in
[
45
]
there exist integral dominant weights
\mu_{p}
,
p=1,\ldots,D
, and elements
v_{p}\in V_{\mu_{p}}
such that
(v_{p},\cdot v_{\mu_{p}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, and

(v_{p},\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})})v_{\mu_{p}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&{\rm if}~f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}=f_{\beta_{p}}\\
0&{\rm otherwise}\end{array}\right.,

(4.4.1)

where the highest weight vectors
v_{\mu_{p}}\in V_{\mu_{p}}
are normalized by the condition
(v_{\mu_{p}},v_{\mu_{p}})=1
.

This definition implies
\omega(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{p}}))v_{p}=v_{\mu_{p}}
, i.e.
v_{p}
has weight
\mu_{p}-\beta_{p}
,
(v_{\mu_{p}},\cdot v_{\mu_{p}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, and

(v_{p},\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{p}})\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=(v_{\mu_{p}},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}).

(4.4.2)

Let

A_{p}=(v_{p},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}),A_{p}^{0}=(v_{\mu_{p}},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}).

(4.4.3)

From (
4.2.2
) we deduce

(v_{\mu_{p}},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})\otimes(v_{p},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=q^{((-\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\beta_{p}^{\vee},\mu_{p}^{\vee})}(v_{p},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})\otimes(v_{\mu_{p}},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}),

i.e.

A_{p}^{0}\otimes A_{p}=q^{((-\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\beta_{p}^{\vee},\mu_{p}^{\vee})}A_{p}\otimes A_{p}^{0}.

(4.4.4)

Now we can define
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and characterize it as a vanishing ideal.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

For
p=1,\ldots,c+1
, let
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and by
A_{1},\ldots,A_{p-1}
.
Let
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements vanishing on
xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime\prime}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements vanishing on
x\overline{T}_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Denote
{\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime\prime}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. Then
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime\prime}
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime\prime}
, and hence
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Moreover,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
,
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime\prime}
, where
\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
.

Proof.

Firstly we claim that
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.
Indeed, any element from
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
belongs to
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}\supset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
as by Lemma
4.3.2

{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Next, for
q<p
and
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
we have, since
v_{\mu_{q}}
is a highest weight vector,

A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})=(v_{q},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{q}})=(v_{q},x\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(y)v_{\mu_{q}})

(4.4.5)

for some
y\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. We claim that that the right hand side of (
4.4.5
) vanishes.

Indeed, if
x\neq 1
then
x\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(y)=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y)
and
f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y
belongs to the right ideal in
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
generated by
f_{\beta_{u}}^{(n_{u})}
,
u\geq p>q
and
n_{u}>0
. So by (
4.4.1
) and by Lemma
2.7.2
we have

A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})=(v_{q},\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y)v_{\mu_{q}})=0

in this case.

If
x=1
we can assume without loss of generality that
z_{+}
and
x^{\prime}
are monomials in quantum root vectors. From (
4.4.1
) it follows that
A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})\neq 0
only if
A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})=(v_{q},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{q}})=(v_{q},c\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{q}})v_{\mu_{q}})
for some
c\in\mathcal{B}
,
c\neq 0
.

Now observe that from the definitions of
\Delta_{0}
and
-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}
it follows that the weight of the element
T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}T_{s^{-1}}
is a linear combination of simple roots from
\Delta_{+}^{s}
with non-negative integer coefficients, and this collection of weights does not contain
-\beta_{q}\in\Delta_{-}^{s}
, the weight of
\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{q}})
. Therefore
A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})=0
in all cases.

We deduce that the elements generating
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
obey the defining property of
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
, and hence
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Observe that by Lemma
4.3.2

{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\overline{J}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
\overline{J}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{-}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
, and by the definition
A_{n}=\phi_{n}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1)\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
for any
n
. Hence, since
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is generated by
{\overline{J}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and by
A_{1},\ldots,A_{p-1}
, Corollary
3.5.5
implies that
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, where
\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
,

All derivatives of elements of
\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
in the directions tangent to
N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}
must vanish. Therefore every element of
\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
must vanish on elements
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}
,
z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
.

Conversely, any element of
\mathbb{C}[G]
vanishing on
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})},z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
must vanish on
N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}
, and hence on
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHM_{-}^{s}}
. Thus
\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
coincides with the ideal in
\mathbb{C}[G]
generated by the regular functions vanishing on
xshz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=X_{-\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots X_{-\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})},z_{+}\in U(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U(\mathfrak{h})
. Therefore
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\overline{J}^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
. Recalling that
{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
we deduce with the help of Lemma
4.3.1
that
{\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}
.

The case of the left ideal generated by the elements of
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
vanishing on elements
x\overline{T}_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
is treated in a similar way since by Lemma
2.7.4
for a highest weight vector
v_{\mu}\in V_{\mu}
there is a non–zero multiple
k
of a power of
q
such that
T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu}=k\overline{T}_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu}
.

Finally observe that
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
as clearly
\overline{J}^{p}_{q}
is a left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{q}[G]
and
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. This completes the proof.

∎

Similarly one can define quantum analogues
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
of the vanishing ideals of the varieties

\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

For
p=1,\ldots,c+1
, let
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by
{J^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and by
A_{1},\ldots,A_{p-1}
.
Let
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
be the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
generated by the elements vanishing on
xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}
with arbitrary
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
. Denote
{J^{p}_{q}}={J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
,
{J^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}={J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
and let
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{p}_{q}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. Then
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
,
{J^{p}_{q}}={J^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}
, and hence
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Moreover,
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=J^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
, where
J^{p}_{1}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Firstly as in the proof of Lemma
4.4.1
we deduce that
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
4.4.1
we also obtain, using the second assertion in Corollary
3.5.5
, that
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}={J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}=J^{p}_{1}
mod
(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}}-1){J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
, where
J^{p}_{1}
is the vanishing ideal of
\overline{N_{[-\beta_{p},-\beta_{D}]}sZHN_{-}^{\prime}}
, and hence by Lemma
4.3.1

{J^{p}_{q}}={J^{p}_{q}}^{\prime}
.

Finally using the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
4.4.1
we deduce that
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.
∎

We conclude this section with a technical lemma which will be used to cancel some terms in formula (
4.2.4
) for the adjoint action.

Lemma 4.4.3
.

For any
n>0
,
A_{p}(\cdot f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)})\in{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
for
q\geq p
,
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
,
q\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
and

A_{p}(\cdot f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)})\in{\overline{J}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
for
q\geq p
,
q\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
.

Proof.

We prove the first statement. The second one can be justified in a similar way with the help of Lemma
4.4.1
.

For
q\geq p
,
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
and any
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}),z_{+}\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+}),x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
we have, since
v_{\mu_{p}}
is a highest weight vector,

(v_{p},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=(v_{p},x\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(y)v_{\mu_{p}})

for some
y\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
. We claim that

(v_{p},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=0

if
x\neq 1,\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{p}})
.

Indeed, let
x\neq 1,\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{p}})
, so
x\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(y)=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y)
. Using commutation relations (
2.7.11
) the element
f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y
can be uniquely represented as a
\mathbb{C}(q^{\frac{1}{dr^{2}}})
–linear combination of monomials
f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}
with
n_{p}>1
or at least one
n_{t}>0
for
t>p
. This presentation is unique by the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt decomposition for
U_{q}^{s}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-})
, and by the uniqueness of the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt decomposition for
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-})
the coefficients in this decomposition belong to
\mathcal{B}
(see Lemma
2.7.2
).

So by (
4.4.1
) we have

A_{q}(xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime})=(v_{q},\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})}y)v_{\mu_{q}})=0

in this case.

If
x=1
then

(v_{p},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=(v_{p},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=c(v_{p},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}),c\in\mathcal{B},

(4.4.6)

and if
x=S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{p}})
then

(v_{p},xT_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=(v_{\mu_{p}},T_{s}hz_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})=c^{\prime}(v_{\mu_{p}},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}}),c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}.

(4.4.7)

We claim that in both cases the right hand side in the formulas above vanishes.
This is obviously the case if
s\beta_{q}\in\Delta_{-}
.
Suppose that
\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{i_{j}}
and
s\beta_{q}\in\Delta_{+}
. Without loss of generality we can assume that
z_{+}
and
x^{\prime}
are monomials in quantum root vectors corresponding to roots from
\Delta_{0}^{+}
and
-\Delta_{s}^{\prime}
, respectively, so
z_{+}x^{\prime}=({X}^{+}_{\delta_{a}})^{(r_{a})}\ldots({X}^{+}_{\delta_{b}})^{(r_{b})}({X}^{-}_{\epsilon_{g}})^{(t_{g})}\ldots({X}^{-}_{\epsilon_{h}})^{(t_{h})}
,
\delta_{a},\ldots,\delta_{b}\in\Delta_{0}^{+}\subset\Delta_{0}
,
\epsilon_{g},\ldots,\epsilon_{h}\in\Delta_{s}^{\prime}
. If
(v_{p},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})
is not zero then the weight of the element
T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}
must be equal to
-\beta_{p}
as the weight of
v_{p}
is
\mu_{p}-\beta_{p}
and if
(v_{\mu_{p}},T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu_{p}})
is not zero then the weight of the element
T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}
must be equal to zero.

Let
k
be minimal possible such that
\beta_{q},\epsilon_{g},\ldots\epsilon_{h}\in\overline{\Delta}_{i_{k}}
.
Denote by
\lambda=r_{a}\delta_{a}+\ldots+r_{b}\delta_{b}-t_{g}\epsilon_{g}-\ldots-t_{h}\epsilon_{h}-n\beta_{q}
the weight of
z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}
. Note that
i_{k}>0
as
q\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
and hence
\beta_{q}\not\in\Delta_{0}
.

The corresponding plane
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
is shown at Figure 6 where we use the same notation as at Figure 2, Section
1.2
. We denote by
\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
the open sector which is transformed into the lower half plane under the action of
s^{-1}
, by
s\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
its image under the action of
s
, and by
-\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
,
-s\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
their images under the multiplication by
-1
. We also denote by
\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
the sector which appeared at Figure 2 under the same name, by
s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
its image under the action of
s
, and by
-s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
its image under the multiplication by
-1
. The orthogonal projections of the roots from the set
\Delta_{0}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
are zeroes and by the definition of the set
\Delta_{s}^{\prime}
and by the properties of normal ordering (
1.6.3
) of the root system
\Delta_{+}^{s}
in Proposition
1.6.1
the orthogonal projections of the roots
-s\epsilon_{g},\ldots,-s\epsilon_{h}\in s(-\Delta_{s}^{\prime})
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
belong to the sectors labeled
\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and
s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
at Figure 6.

Since
\beta_{q}\geq\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
and
\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
is an initial segment in
\Delta_{+}^{s}
we also have
\beta_{q}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
and also by the assumption
s\beta_{q}\in\Delta_{+}
. Therefore the orthogonal projection of
-s\beta_{q}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
belongs to the lower half plane or possibly to the sector labeled
\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
at Figure 6 and does not belong to the sectors labeled
-\Delta_{s^{-1}}
and
-s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
at Figure 6 This projection is labeled
-s\beta_{q}
at Figure 6.

If the orthogonal projection of
-s\beta_{q}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
belongs to the sector labeled
\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
at Figure 6 then the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
belongs to the interior of the closure of the union of the sectors
\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and
s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
, and the intersection of this union with the lower half plane is at most
-\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
. Remark that in this case the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
is not zero, and hence
\lambda\neq 0
.

If
k=j
note that the orthogonal projection of
-\beta_{p}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{j}}
belongs to the open lower half plane and does not belong to
-\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
. Thus in this case
s\lambda\neq-\beta_{p}
.

If the orthogonal projection of
-s\beta_{q}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
belongs to the lower half plane and does not belong to the sectors labeled
-\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and
-s\Delta_{i_{k}}^{1}
at Figure 6 then the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{j}}
belongs to the closed left half plane bounded by the dashed line at Figure 6. This projection labeled
s\lambda
is shown at Figure 6 as well. Observe that in this case the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
is not zero, and hence
\lambda\neq 0
.

If
k=j
, recall that
\beta_{p}\leq\beta_{q}
where
\beta_{p},\beta_{q}\in{\Delta}_{i_{j}}
, and hence by the last property of the normal ordering in
\Delta_{+}^{s}
in Proposition
1.6.1
the orthogonal projection of
-\beta_{p}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{j}}
labeled by the same letter at Figure 6 belongs to the other open half plane bounded by the dashed line at Figure 6. Thus again
s\lambda\neq-\beta_{p}
.

In all cases above,
if
k>j
then the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
is not zero while the orthogonal projection of
-\beta_{p}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{k}}
is zero, and hence
s\lambda\neq-\beta_{p}
.
If
k<j
then the orthogonal projection of
s\lambda
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{j}}
is zero while the orthogonal projection of
-\beta_{p}
onto
\mathfrak{h}_{i_{j}}
is zero, and hence again
s\lambda\neq-\beta_{p}
.

We deduce that in all cases the weight of the element
T_{s}z_{+}x^{\prime}f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)}T_{s^{-1}}
is not equal to
-\beta_{p}
or to zero. Therefore the right hand side in formulas (
4.4.6
), (
4.4.7
) vanishes, and by the definition of
{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
in Lemma
4.4.2

A_{p}(\cdot f_{\beta_{q}}^{(n)})\in{J^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}
for
q\geq p
,
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
,
q\in\{1,\ldots,c\}
.

∎

4.5
Localizations

In order co define quantum counterparts of formulas (
3.5.10
) we have to introduce a certain localization
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
of
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. However,
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
is not a localization in the sense of localization for non–commutative algebras, and it is rather impossible to define a proper localization of the algebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
which contains the quantum counterparts of the denominators in formula (
3.5.10
) given by (
4.4.3
). But in fact we shall only need right denominators and
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
will be defined as a “right” localization of
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. The exact meaning of this term will be explained below. Along with
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
we shall also define “right” localizations
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
of
\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}
and
I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}
and study their properties.

We start by introducing a subalgebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
a proper localization of which contains all the required denominators.

Lemma 4.5.1
.

The set of elements
(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
v
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
and
u\in V
form a subalgebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the product of two elements from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
belongs to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
.
Let
s^{-1}=s_{i_{1}}\ldots s_{i_{k}}
be a reduced decomposition of
s^{-1}
. Then by (
2.6.12
)

\displaystyle\Delta_{s}(T_{s^{-1}})=T_{s^{-1}}\otimes T_{s^{-1}}q^{\sum_{i=1}^{l}(-T_{s}Y_{i}\otimes T_{s}KH_{i}+Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i})}\prod_{p=1}^{k}\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{s},

(4.5.1)

where for
p=1,\ldots,k

\beta_{p}^{\prime}=s_{i_{k}}\ldots s_{i_{p+1}}\alpha_{i_{p}},{\overline{X}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{\pm}}=T_{i_{k}}^{-1}\ldots T_{i_{p+1}}^{-1}X_{i_{p}}^{\pm},

\overline{e}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}=\psi_{\{n_{ij}\}}^{-1}(\overline{X}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{+}e^{hK{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}),\overline{f}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}=\psi_{\{n_{ij}\}}^{-1}(e^{-hK{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}\overline{X}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{-}),~K_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}=T_{i_{k}}^{-1}\ldots T_{i_{p+1}}^{-1}K_{i_{p}},

\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{s}=exp_{q_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}}[(1-q_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{-2})K_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}e^{h\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}{\overline{f}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}}\otimes{\overline{e}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}}K_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}].

Since
Kh=\frac{\kappa}{2}{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}h
for any
h\in\mathfrak{h}
, we have
Ks=sK
, and hence

q^{\sum_{i=1}^{l}(-T_{s}Y_{i}\otimes T_{s}KH_{i}+Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i})}=q^{\sum_{i=1}^{l}(-T_{s}Y_{i}\otimes KT_{s}H_{i}+Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i})}=q^{\sum_{i=1}^{l}(-Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i}+Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i})}=1

(4.5.2)

where we also used the identity
\sum_{i=1}^{l}T_{s}Y_{i}\otimes KT_{s}H_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i}
which holds since
T_{s}Y_{i}
,
T_{s}H_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,l
is a pair of dual bases in
\mathfrak{h}
.

Now (
4.5.1
) takes the form

\Delta_{s}(T_{s^{-1}})=T_{s^{-1}}\otimes T_{s^{-1}}\prod_{p=1}^{k}\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}^{\prime}}^{s},

and for two highest weight vectors
v\in V,v^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}
we have

\Delta_{s}(T_{s^{-1}})v\otimes v^{\prime}=T_{s^{-1}}v\otimes T_{s^{-1}}v^{\prime}.

(4.5.3)

Therefore for any
u\in V,u^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}
we have by (
4.5.3
)

(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v)\otimes(u^{\prime},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v^{\prime})=(u\otimes u^{\prime},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v\otimes T_{s^{-1}}v^{\prime})=(u\otimes u^{\prime},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}(v\otimes v^{\prime})).

(4.5.4)

Since
v\otimes v^{\prime}
is a highest weight vector in
V\otimes V^{\prime}
, the last identity implies
(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v)\otimes(u^{\prime},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
. This completes the proof.

∎

From formulas (
4.2.2
) and (
4.5.4
) it follows that the set
S=\{\Delta_{\mu}=(v_{\mu},\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v_{\mu})\}|\mu\in P_{+}\}
, where
v_{\mu}\in V_{\mu}
are highest weight vectors normalized by
(v_{\mu},v_{\mu})=1
, is a multiplicative set of normal elements in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
.
Let
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
be the localization of
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
by
S
.

We shall need more information on the structure of subalgebras
N_{k}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
generated by the elements

B_{p}={A_{p}^{0}}^{-1}\otimes A_{p}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0},

(4.5.5)

p=1,\ldots,k
. The elements
B_{p}
are quantum analogues of functions (
3.5.10
).
The following Lemma is similar to Proposition 8.3 in
[
45
]
.

Lemma 4.5.2
.

Let
N_{k}
be the subalgebra in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
generated by the elements
B_{p}={A_{p}^{0}}^{-1}\otimes A_{p}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
,
p=1,\ldots,k
, and
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}
the subalgebra in
{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathcal{B}}[G_{*}]
generated by the elements
e_{p}=(q_{\beta_{p}}^{-1}-q_{\beta_{p}})q^{\beta_{p}^{\vee}}e_{\beta_{p}}
,
p=1,\ldots,k
. Then the map

B_{p}\mapsto e_{p}

gives rise to an algebra isomorphism
\vartheta:N_{k}\rightarrow U_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}
.

In particular, the elements
B_{p}
satisfy the following commutation relations

B_{p}B_{r}-q^{(\beta_{p},\beta_{r})+(\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime*}}\beta_{p},\beta_{r})}B_{r}B_{p}=\sum_{k_{p+1},\ldots,k_{r-1}}C(k_{p+1},\ldots,k_{r-1})B_{p+1}^{k_{p+1}}\ldots B_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}},~p<r,

(4.5.6)

where
C(k_{p+1},\ldots,k_{r-1})\in\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

First observe that the map

\psi:\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathcal{B}}[B_{-}],(u,\cdot v)\mapsto(u\otimes id,(\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R})v\otimes id)

is an algebra homomorphism. Indeed, since
\omega_{0}
is a coautomorphism

(\Delta_{s}\otimes id)((\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}^{s}))=(\omega_{0}\otimes\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\Delta_{s}\otimes id)\mathcal{R}^{s}=(\omega_{0}\otimes\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}_{13}^{s}\mathcal{R}_{23}^{s})=(\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}_{13}^{s})(\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}_{23}^{s}),

and hence

\psi((u,\cdot v)\otimes(u^{\prime},\cdot v^{\prime}))=(u\otimes id,(\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}_{13}^{s})v\otimes id)(u^{\prime}\otimes id,(\omega_{0}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R}_{23}^{s})v^{\prime}\otimes id)=\psi((u,\cdot v))\psi((u^{\prime},\cdot v^{\prime})),

i.e.
\psi
is an algebra homomorphism.

Recall that by Lemma
4.5.1
elements of the form
(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
v
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
and
u\in V
, form a subalgebra
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

From (
4.5.3
) we also deduce that the map

\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G],(u,\cdot T_{s^{-1}}v)\mapsto(u,\cdot v)

is an algebra homomorphism.
Composing this map with
\psi
we obtain another algebra homomorphism
\psi^{0}
.

Next using (
4.1.5
) and the definition of
A_{p}
we obtain

\psi^{0}(A_{p})=(v_{p}\otimes id,(\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}\otimes id)(\mathcal{R})v_{\mu_{p}}\otimes id)=(v_{p}\otimes id,(\omega_{0}\otimes id)({{\mathcal{R}}^{s}}^{-1})v_{\mu_{p}}\otimes id)=

=(q_{\beta_{p}}^{-1}-q_{\beta_{p}})q^{(\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\mu_{p}^{\vee}}q^{\beta_{p}^{\vee}}e_{\beta_{p}}.

Similarly

\psi^{0}(A_{p}^{0})=q^{(\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}+id)\mu_{p}^{\vee}}.

From the last two formulas we deduce that
\psi^{0}
gives rise to an algebra homomorphism
\vartheta:N_{k}\rightarrow U_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}
such that

\vartheta(B_{p})=\vartheta({A_{p}^{0}}^{-1}\otimes A_{p}))=(q_{\beta_{p}}^{-1}-q_{\beta_{p}})q^{\beta_{p}^{\vee}}e_{\beta_{p}}=e_{p}.

This homomorphism is surjective by construction.
\vartheta
is also injective as
\psi^{0}
is injective (see Proposition 8.3 in
[
45
]
and
[
118
]
, Theorem 2.6 for the proof).

Commutation relations (
4.5.6
) follow from (
3.2.10
) by applying
\omega
and by multiplying by
q^{\beta_{p}^{\vee}}q^{\beta_{r}^{\vee}}
.

∎

Denote
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]=\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
.
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
is naturally a left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–module and a right
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
–module. We denote by
\otimes

both the left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–action and the right
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
–action on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
and call these action multiplications.

Let
S^{*}=\{\Delta_{\lambda}\otimes\Delta_{\mu}^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}|\lambda,\mu\in P_{+}\}
,
S^{-1}=\{\Delta_{\mu}^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}|\mu\in P_{+}\}
,
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
the images of
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes S^{*}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
and of
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes S^{*}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
, respectively, in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
under the projection
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]_{0}}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}=\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
.

For
p=1,\ldots,c+1
let

\mathbb{C}_{p}^{loc}[G]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}~~{\rm if}~~\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\\
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}~~{\rm if}~~\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}\end{array}\right..

\mathbb{C}_{p}^{loc}[G]
is naturally a left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–module.

Note that
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}\subset{\overline{I}^{p+1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
,
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}\subset{I^{p+1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
for
p=1,\ldots,c
, and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}\subset{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
for
p=1,\ldots,c+1
.

Here and everywhere below for the technical reasons which already became manifest in Lemma
4.3.7
we have to distinguish two cases in our consideration,
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
and
\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
. Conceptually this complication is related to the non–commutativity of the multiplication in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
.

By the definition
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
are left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–submodules in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]
.
The following lemma shows that
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
,
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
,
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
have some nice properties with respect to multiplication by the elements
A_{r},\Delta_{\mu},B_{r}\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]_{0}
from the right as well.

Lemma 4.5.3
.

For any
p\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}

{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
is invariant with respect to multiplication from the right by
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
, and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}A_{r}={I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}B_{r}\subset{I^{r}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
for any
r>p
,
r\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
.

For any
p\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
and any
\mu\in P_{+}

{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is invariant with respect to multiplication from the right by
\Delta_{\mu}
.

For any
p\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
such that
\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}

{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
is invariant with respect to multiplication from the right by
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
, and for
\beta_{r}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and any
r>p
,
r\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
we have
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}A_{r}={\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}B_{r}\subset{\overline{I}^{r}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
.

For any
p\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
such that
\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
and any
\mu\in P_{+}

{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is invariant with respect to multiplication from the right by
\Delta_{\mu}
.

Thus multiplication from the right induces a natural action of
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
on
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{loc}[G]
and for
r\geq p
,
r\in\{1,\ldots,c+1\}
right multiplication by
A_{r}
or
B_{r}
gives rise to well–defined homomorphisms of left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–modules
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{I^{r}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
, and, if in addition
\beta_{r}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
, right multiplication by
A_{r}
or
B_{r}
gives rise to well–defined homomorphisms of left
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
–modules
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{\overline{I}^{r}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
.

Proof.

The proof of the first claim follows from the definitions of
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
and
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
, commutation relations (
4.2.2
), (
4.5.6
) and from the fact that
{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a two–sided ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
. For the same reason
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is invariant with respect to the multiplication from the right by
\Delta_{\mu}
for any
\mu\in P_{+}
.

From commutation relations (
4.2.2
), (
4.5.6
) it also follows that to establish the second claim it suffices to show that for
r\leq p
with
\beta_{p}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
we have
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}A_{r}\subset\overline{J}^{p}_{q}
, and to establish the last claim it suffices to verify that
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}\Delta_{\mu}\subset{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}
for any
\mu\in P_{+}
.

Indeed, recall that
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}
is the left ideal in
\mathbb{C}_{q}[G]
generated by the elements
(u,\cdot v)\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
, where
u
is a highest weight vector in a finite rank representation
V
of
U_{h}(\mathfrak{g})
, and
v\in V
is such that
(u,\overline{T}_{s}xv)=0
for any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
.

From formula (
2.6.13
) with
k
equal to the length of
s
we have

\Delta_{s}(\overline{T}_{s})=\prod^{k}_{p=1}{\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}}^{s}}^{\prime}q^{\sum_{i=1}^{l}(-Y_{i}\otimes KH_{i}+T_{s}Y_{i}\otimes T_{s}KH_{i})}\overline{T}_{s}\otimes\overline{T}_{s}=\prod^{k}_{p=1}{\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}}^{s}}^{\prime}\overline{T}_{s}\otimes\overline{T}_{s}

(4.5.7)

where we used (
4.5.2
).

Let
y=x\overline{T}_{s}hzx^{\prime}
,
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{D}}^{(n_{D})}\ldots f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n_{p})})
,
z\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
.
Since
u
is a highest weight vector we obtain from (
4.5.7
), the definition of
{\overline{\theta}_{\beta_{p}}^{s}}^{\prime}
after (
2.6.13
), (
2.7.12
) and the fact that
\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}
is an anticoautomorphism that

((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=(u,\overline{T}_{s}h_{1}z_{1}x^{\prime}_{1}v)(v_{r},x\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}T_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}})=

=t(u,\overline{T}_{s}h_{1}z_{1}x^{\prime}_{1}v)(v_{r},x\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}}),

where
t
is a non–zero multiple of a power of
q
defined in Lemma
2.7.4
, and we use the Sweedler notation for the coproducts, so for any
a\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{g})\subset U_{\mathcal{B}}^{res}(\mathfrak{g})\simeq U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{g})

\Delta_{s}(a)=a_{1}\otimes a_{2}
.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.4.1
(see the discussion after formula (
4.4.5
)) we also deduce that
(v_{r},x\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}})=0
if
x\neq 1,\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{r}})
, i.e
((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=0
if
x\neq 1,\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{r}})
.

Let
x=1
. Recall that
e_{\beta}=X_{\beta}^{+}q^{K\beta^{\vee}}
and
f_{\beta}=q^{-K\beta^{\vee}}X_{\beta}^{-}
, and hence

\Delta_{s}(X_{\beta}^{+})=\Delta_{s}(e_{\beta})q^{-K\beta^{\vee}}\otimes q^{-K\beta^{\vee}},\Delta_{s}(X_{\beta}^{-})=q^{K\beta^{\vee}}\otimes q^{K\beta^{\vee}}\Delta_{s}(f_{\beta}).

(4.5.8)

Combining these expressions with formulas (
2.7.12
) and (
2.7.14
) we deduce that

h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{h})U_{q}^{res}([\Delta_{0}^{+},-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}]),

where
[\Delta_{0}^{+},-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}]
is the minimal segment in
\Delta
containing
\Delta_{0}^{+}
as its initial segment and
-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}
is its last element. But
\beta_{r}\in\Delta_{s^{-1}}^{s}
, so
s^{-1}(-\beta_{r})\in\Delta_{s}^{s}
. Note also that
[\Delta_{0}^{+},-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}]
is a subset of a system of positive roots
\Delta_{0}^{+}\cup(\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{-})
and
\Delta_{s}^{s}\cap(\Delta_{0}^{+}\cup(\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{-}))=\emptyset
. Therefore the element
\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}\overline{T}_{s^{-1}}
has no components of weight
-\beta_{r}
in its weight decomposition. Since the weight of
v_{r}
is
\mu_{r}-\beta_{r}
we deduce that

((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=(u,\overline{T}_{s}h_{1}z_{1}x^{\prime}_{1}v)(v_{r},\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}})=0

in this case.

If
x=\omega_{0}S_{s}^{-1}(f_{\beta_{r}})
we have by the definition of
v_{r}

((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=(u,\overline{T}_{s}h_{1}z_{1}x^{\prime}_{1}v)(v_{\mu_{r}},\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}}),

(4.5.9)

and hence only zero weight components of the element
h_{2}z_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}
will contribute to the right hand side of the formula above. Recall that
h_{2}z_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}\in U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{h})U_{q}^{res}([\Delta_{0}^{+},-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}])
and as we observed above
[\Delta_{0}^{+},-\gamma_{l^{\prime}}]
is a subset of the system of positive roots
\Delta_{0}^{+}\cup(\Delta_{-}^{s}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{-})
. Therefore only zero degree monomials in quantum root vectors in the decomposition of
h_{2}z_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}
will contribute to the right hand side of (
4.5.9
). If we assume, without loss of generality, that
z
and
x^{\prime}
are monomials in quantum root vectors then from (
4.5.8
), (
2.7.12
) and (
2.7.14
) one can see that the above mentioned contribution amounts to

((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=(u,\overline{T}_{s}h_{1}z_{1}x^{\prime}_{1}v)(v_{\mu_{r}},\overline{T}_{s}h_{2}z_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}\overline{T}_{s}^{-1}v_{\mu_{r}})=c^{\prime}(u,\overline{T}_{s}zx^{\prime}v),c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}.

Finally observe that
zx^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{b}_{+}))
, and hence
(u,\overline{T}_{s}zx^{\prime}v)=t^{\prime}(u,{T}_{s}zx^{\prime}v)=0
by the choice of
u
and
v
. Here
t^{\prime}
is a non–zero multiple of a power of
q
given by Lemma
2.7.4
.

So in all cases
((u,\cdot v)\otimes A_{r})(y)=0
, and hence
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}A_{r}\subset\overline{J}^{p}_{q}
by Lemma
4.4.1
.

Similar arguments show that for any
\mu\in P_{+}
and
y=x\overline{T}_{s}hzx^{\prime}
with any
x\in U_{q}^{res}(\mathfrak{n}_{-})
,
z\in U_{q}^{res}(\Delta_{0}^{+})
,
x^{\prime}\in U_{q}^{res}(-\Delta_{\mathfrak{m}_{+}}^{s}),h\in U_{q}^{res}(H)
one has

((u,\cdot v)\otimes\Delta_{\mu})(y)=0,

and hence
{\overline{J}^{1}_{q}}
is invariant with respect to multiplication from the right by
\Delta_{\mu}
for any
\mu\in P_{+}
by Lemma
4.3.2
.

∎

In the next two lemmas we study the properties of
{\overline{I}^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
,
{I^{p}_{\mathcal{B}}}
, and
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{loc}[G]
with respect to the adjoint action. These properties will be needed to study properties of quantum analogues of the operators
\Pi_{p}
defined in (
3.5.11
).

Lemma 4.5.4
.

For any
p=1,\ldots,c
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
f\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
we have

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f\otimes\Delta_{\mu})=q^{n((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\beta_{p}^{\vee},\mu^{\vee})}{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f)\otimes\Delta_{\mu}

(4.5.10)

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
. Thus the adjoint action of
U_{\mathcal{B}}^{s,res}(\mathfrak{m}_{-})
on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
defined in Lemma
4.3.2
induces an action on
\mathbb{C}_{1}^{loc}[G]
satisfying

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f\otimes\Delta_{\mu}^{-1})=q^{-n((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\beta_{p}^{\vee},\mu^{\vee})}{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f)\otimes\Delta_{\mu}^{-1},f\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{\overline{I}^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}=\mathbb{C}_{1}^{loc}[G].

(4.5.11)

This action is locally finite.

For any
p=1,\ldots,c
with
\beta_{p}\not\in\Delta_{s^{1}}^{s}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
f\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]
we have

{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f\otimes\Delta_{\mu})=q^{n((\kappa{1+s\over 1-s}P_{{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}}-id)\beta_{p}^{\vee},\mu^{\vee})}{\rm Ad}_{0}f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}(f)\otimes\Delta_{\mu}

(4.5.12)

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
. Thus the adjoint action of
f_{\beta_{p}}^{(n)}
on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}[G]/{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}
defined in Lemma
4.3.5
induces a locally finite action on
\mathbb{C}_{\mathcal{B}}^{loc}[G]/{I^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{loc}
satisfying
